### Page 71

Australian GDP growth is expected
to remain below our estimate of
potential growth throughout the
forecast period.
GDP growth has slowed since the end of 2025 and is
expected to remain subdued over the next couple of
years (Graph 3.3). The expected slowing in growth over
2026 reflects the effects of several headwinds weighing
on private activity, including the reduction in household
disposable incomes from higher inflation, weaker
conditions in the established housing market, and the

tightening in monetary policy, which more than offset
very strong data centre-related investment. Growth is
expected to remain below potential over the forecast
period, reflecting an assumption that financial
conditions remain restrictive (see Key judgement #2).
Consistent with the latest state and federal budgets,
public demand is expected to continue supporting
growth over the forecast period.
The risks to activity appear to be broadly balanced.

Stronger-than-expected global growth and further
strength in AI and data centre-related activity pose
upside risks to domestic growth (see Key risk #2).
However, historically low consumer and business
sentiment could be pointing to the risk that the
combined effect on domestic activity of the Middle East
conflict, the decline in housing prices and the tightening
in monetary policy could be more than assumed (see
Key risk #3).
The level of GDP at end-2028 is expected to be

broadly unchanged from the August forecasts.
Growth is projected to be slightly stronger than
expected in the near term, reflecting a much stronger
outlook for data centre-related investment compared
with the August Statement. Weighing against that is the
higher assumed market path for the cash rate, a weaker
outlook for the housing market and higher oil prices.
These pull growth in the latter part of the forecast period
a little below that expected in the August Statement.

Business investment is expected to increase strongly
and the outlook has been revised up significantly,

largely driven by data centre-related investment.
Business investment was stronger than expected in the
June quarter and firms’ surveyed expectations of future
investment were again revised higher. External
projections for the expected increase in Australia’s data
centre capacity through to 2030 have also continued to
be revised up as the size of the development pipeline
has increased. Our forecast for data centre investment is
in line with recent baseline external projections, but

below projections that assume Australia becomes a
significant regional hub for data centres. There is
considerable uncertainty around the extent of data
centre investment over coming years. This will depend
on a range of factors such as government policy,
community expectations, future demand for AI services
and grid capacity. We continue to expect that much of
this additional investment will be imported (around
70 per cent), limiting the boost to GDP growth. However,

data centre construction, as well as any potential
spillovers to higher investment in energy and utilities
infrastructure, will require domestically sourced labour
and materials. At this stage, most of this investment
pipeline is in New South Wales and Victoria where liaison
information suggests capacity constraints in the
construction sector are somewhat less binding.
3.3 Domestic outlook

At the same time, a higher assumed cash rate path, a
more significant easing in the established housing
market and higher global oil prices are expected to
weigh more on activity than in the August forecasts.
The forecasts are conditioned on a higher market path
for interest rates relative to August. In addition,
established housing prices have continued to fall more
quickly than expected, with external forecasters now
expecting a deeper downturn than assumed a few

months ago. The outlook for oil prices is also much
higher than assumed in August, which will weigh on real
household incomes. Reflecting these factors, the outlook
for household consumption and dwelling investment
has been revised lower, as has the outlook for business
investment unrelated to data centres.
The labour market is expected to
continue easing over the forecast
period, consistent with
below-potential GDP growth.
The unemployment rate is forecast to increase to

5.1 per cent by the end of 2028, which is around
1⁄4 percentage point higher than previously
expected (Graph 3.4). The unemployment rate has risen
by more than expected over recent months, and the
upward revision to forecast relative to the August
Statement largely reflects this higher starting point.
Elevated cost-of-living pressures appear to have
encouraged workers to enter or remain in the labour
force to a greater extent in recent months than was

expected at the time of the August Statement. These
pressures are expected to continue to support
participation for some time. The participation rate is
expected to decline very modestly by the end of 2028 as
easing cyclical pressures are expected to more than
offset the longer-run upward trend in female
participation. A slightly weaker outlook for demand later
in the forecast period is also contributing a little to the
upward revision to the unemployment rate profile by

late 2028.

As in previous forecasts, the
employment-to-population ratio is expected to
decline through to late 2028. Employment growth is

expected to remain broadly stable over the next year, in
line with the leading indicators of labour demand. From
mid-2027, employment growth is expected to be a little
weaker than in the August Statement, reflecting the
weaker outlook for GDP growth later in the forecast
period. The forecast employment-to-population ratio is
similar to the August Statement in the near term, before
declining more quickly to reach a lower level by the end
of the forecast period.

The economy is expected to move
into balance in 2027, with spare
capacity then emerging in the
economy over the rest of the forecast
period.
Below-potential output growth and an easing in
labour market conditions are expected to see the
economy move from a position of excess demand to
around balance in 2027, before some spare capacity
emerges. The timing of the return to balance is slightly
later than in the August Statement, as a result of the
upgrade to near-term GDP growth.

However, there is considerable uncertainty around
the assessment of when the economy will return to
balance and the extent of spare capacity that
emerges. The impact of conflict-related costs on
inflation over the period ahead will also make it more
difficult to assess the degree of underlying capacity
pressures in the domestic economy. Our assumption for
potential output growth over the next couple of years
assumes productivity growth will pick up gradually (see

Key risk #4). However, with the level of labour
productivity remaining around the same rate as prior to
the pandemic – implying near-zero productivity growth
on average over that period – continued weak
productivity outcomes may be constraining the ability of
firms to grow their supply capacity without generating
inflationary pressure.
Wages growth is expected to pick up
a little in the near term, then
moderate gradually from 2027 as
labour market conditions ease.

The forecast for year-ended wages growth has been
revised a little higher across the forecast period. In
the near term, nominal wages growth is expected to be
supported by the effect of higher-than-expected
capacity pressures in the first half of the year as well as
the Fair Work Commission’s award wage decision (which
had already been incorporated in the August forecasts).
The influence of both higher-than-expected inflation
outcomes and inflation expectations on wage

negotiations is expected to add further upward pressure
on wages growth relative to the August forecast. Some
large wage increases in enterprise bargaining
agreements are expected to support wages growth in
the near term, although the timing of pay increases for
these agreements may contribute to volatility in
quarterly outcomes, particularly for the public sector.
Wages growth expectations from firms in the RBA’s
liaison program are elevated and suggest little

easing over the year ahead (see Box C: Insights from
Liaison). This is consistent with the central forecast for
wages growth. Growth is expected to moderate from
mid-2027 onwards, reflecting easing labour market
conditions and lower inflation outcomes. Wages growth
is expected to have eased to 2.9 per cent in year-ended
terms by end-2028 (Graph 3.5).

Unit labour cost growth is forecast to
be elevated in the near term, before
easing over the second half of the
forecast period.
Year-ended growth in unit labour costs (ULCs) is
expected to increase in coming quarters, reflecting
weak productivity growth and higher wages growth.
Year-ended growth in ULCs is then expected to
moderate from late 2027 onwards, alongside slowing
nominal wages growth and the pick-up in productivity
growth to its assumed medium-term trend growth rate

of 0.7 per cent per year (Graph 3.6). Nominal ULCs are
the measure of labour costs most relevant for firms’ cost
of production and so for inflation outcomes. Year-ended
ULCs growth is expected to remain above 2.5 per cent –
the rate that is consistent with inflation being
sustainably at target – until late in the forecast period.

The forecast for underlying inflation
has been revised higher over the
next year, mainly owing to higher
conflict-related cost pressures and
higher-than-expected domestic
capacity pressures.
Trimmed mean inflation is expected to remain
above 3 per cent until the end of 2027, easing
gradually to reach 21⁄2 per cent around mid-2028
(Graph 3.7). Year-ended underlying inflation is expected

to remain elevated at a little above 31⁄2 per cent until
mid-2027, reflecting existing capacity pressures in the
economy and the pass-through of higher
conflict-related costs to consumer prices. Elevated
inflation expectations are also contributing to
underlying inflation over the coming year or so. From
mid-2027 onwards, inflation is expected to ease due to
the emergence of spare capacity in the economy and
labour market and as conflict-related cost effects begin

to unwind.

Cost pressures related to the Middle East conflict are
expected to place more upward pressure on
underlying inflation over the year ahead than
previously assumed. A possible re-escalation in the
conflict had been flagged as an upside risk to inflation in
the August Statement. Since then, oil and fuel prices
have increased by much more than had been assumed
and are now assumed to remain at a higher level over
the forecast period. We expect higher global energy

prices will contribute to higher domestic inflationary
pressures in late 2026 and early 2027, even as domestic
activity slows and the labour market eases (Graph 3.8).
The judgement around the degree of pass-through from
global energy prices to domestic prices is broadly similar
to what had been assumed in August. It is consistent
with information from liaison and the Business Outlook
Scenario Survey (BOSS) in September, where firms
reported plans to continue raising prices in response to

past cost increases arising partially from the conflict.
From mid-2027 onwards, we expect cost effects to
unwind in net terms, albeit to a lesser extent than
assumed in the August Statement as oil prices are now
assumed to remain at a higher level than previously
expected and refined margins are assumed to remain
elevated for longer (see Key judgement #1). The balance
of risks to conflict-related inflationary pressures remains
skewed to the upside, particularly as crude oil and

refined fuel prices could remain elevated or rise further
(see Key risk #1).
Relative to the August forecasts, domestic capacity
pressures are also expected to exert a bit more
upward pressure on inflation over the year ahead.
We now judge that stronger-than-expected domestic
capacity pressures explain more of the recent
unexpected strength in underlying inflation (see
Chapter 2: Economic Conditions). Those higher capacity
pressures are also expected to contribute a little more to

inflation over the year ahead. This upward pressure is
expected to ease gradually over the forecast period, as
spare capacity emerges in the economy and the
labour market.

Elevated inflation expectations are expected to

contribute to inflationary pressure over the forecast
period, supporting the pass-through of firms’ costs
to consumer prices. Several measures of short- and
medium-term inflation expectations have increased
alongside the recent increases in fuel prices, and some
measures of longer-term expectations have also drifted
higher (see Chapter 2: Economic Conditions). We judge
that elevated expectations will contribute to higher
realised inflation by supporting expected cost growth,

making firms more willing to pass these higher costs
through to prices, and placing upward pressure on
wages growth. If the conflict in the Middle East is more
protracted than assumed in our baseline forecasts, there
is a risk that inflation expectations may increase further
(see Key risk #1).

Headline inflation has been revised
higher in the near term due to
stronger growth in fuel prices and
higher underlying inflation.
Headline inflation is expected to remain above 3 per
cent until late 2027, which is around three quarters
longer than in the August forecast, and reach the
midpoint of the 2–3 per cent inflation target range a
bit later than expected in August (Graph 3.9). Headline
inflation is expected to peak at 4.5 per cent in the
December quarter this year due to

higher-than-expected fuel prices. The assumed decline
in global oil prices is expected to put downward
pressure on headline inflation from early 2027. Over the
remainder of the forecast period, headline inflation has
been revised higher relative to our August forecasts,
reflecting higher underlying inflation (Graph 3.10).
Headline inflation is expected to reach the midpoint of
the target in mid-2028 as capacity pressures and
conflict-related costs ease.

### Page 77

The staff assess that the risks to the domestic inflation
outlook are skewed to the upside, despite becoming
more balanced since the August Statement as some of
the risks identified then have been incorporated into the
central forecast. In August, we identified three main
sources of upside risk to the global and domestic
inflation outlook: a re-escalation of the Middle East
conflict; domestic capacity pressures being greater than
assumed; and the global AI boom generating more of an

inflationary impetus than assumed. Since then, the risk
of a re-escalation of the Middle East conflict has
crystallised and staff have re-evaluated the extent of
domestic capacity constraints; both of which have led to
upward revisions to the central inflation forecast over the
next year. However, there is still uncertainty around the
outlook for global energy prices, the role of inflation
expectations in price and wage dynamics as well as the
impact on inflation from AI activity.

The staff also identified in August that there were upside
risks to global and domestic economic activity from
AI-related demand, but that domestic economic activity
could be weaker if the Middle East conflict and the
decline in housing prices affected the economy by more
than assumed in the central forecast. These remain key
risks to the outlook.
Key risk #1 – High inflation could be
more persistent if energy prices
remain elevated, or if inflation

expectations increase or start to
have a greater bearing on wage- and
price-setting.
We have modelled a scenario where crude oil
remains around US$100 per barrel and refined
product prices remain at elevated levels over the
forecast period. Many external forecasters are revising
higher their outlook for crude oil and refined product
prices, on concerns that some of the key margins of
adjustment in global energy markets that have helped
to cushion the impact of the disruption to oil supply and

global activity are reaching their limits (see Chapter 2:
Economic Conditions and Key judgement #1).
Persistently higher global oil prices would leave
domestic inflation higher for a longer period than in
the central forecast. Higher oil prices would directly
increase fuel prices and firms’ input costs, with the latter
gradually passed through to consumer prices as firms
adjust to persistently higher costs. By contrast, the
central forecast assumes a decline in oil prices, which

starts to ease cost pressures related to the Middle East
conflict from mid-2027, contributing to lower inflation
over time (Graph 3.11). Higher inflation also raises
inflation expectations in the scenario, reinforcing
inflationary pressures over the forecast period. Assuming
that the cash rate and exchange rate follow the same
path that underpins the central forecasts, underlying
inflation would be around 23⁄4 per cent by
end-2028 rather than being around the 21⁄2 per cent.

3.4 Key risks to the outlook
Chapter 3  |  OutlookOutlookOutlookOutlookOutlookOutlookOutlook
Reserve Bank of Australia  |  Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026 74

### Page 78

It is possible that inflation expectations could rise
even further or that inflation expectations provide
greater impetus to wages growth and inflation
through second-round effects. The central forecasts
and scenario assume that the increase in households’
and firms’ inflation expectations do exert some upward
pressure on inflation and wages growth. However, given
households and firms are paying particularly close
attention to inflation, it is possible that inflation

expectations will influence price- and wage-setting
decisions by more than assumed. This heightened
attentiveness to inflation could also make expectations
more persistent and more responsive to realised
inflation. With headline inflation forecast to rise further in
the near term, this increases the risk that inflation
expectations remain higher for longer than assumed in
our central forecast.
Key risk #2 – The global AI demand
boom may continue to drive up

global growth and inflation.
MTP growth may not moderate as expected over the
next few years if AI-demand growth continues at the
same pace. It is possible that growth forecasts could
continue to be upwardly revised by Consensus
forecasters if the current upswing in AI-related
investment (and spillovers associated with the boom) is
more persistent than currently assumed. The rise in
interest rates across the yield curve in many advanced
economies may reflect higher neutral rates – that is,

expectations that demand will prove more resilient and
inflation more persistent than currently assumed. Higher
longer-term real rates may partly reflect expectations of
stronger future productivity growth, including from the
adoption of AI. Further investment in anticipation of
higher productivity could also support demand and add
to capacity pressures over the next few years, while most
of the productivity gains are likely to manifest beyond
the next two years (at least in Australia).

The ramp-up of investment in data centres may also
contribute to capacity and pricing pressures
domestically, particularly by drawing on resources in
the construction industry. These price pressures could
be stronger than currently expected as the forecast
pipeline of investment is worked through, or if
investment proves stronger than expected.
Key risk #3 – Domestic economic
activity could be weaker than
expected.
It is possible that the headwinds affecting

households and businesses over the next year could
have a larger adverse effect than assumed. The
central forecasts assume that higher oil prices, higher
interest rates and lower housing prices all weigh on
domestic economic activity broadly in line with average
historical relationships. Timely indicators of spending
remain consistent with this assumption, with no
evidence of a material slowing in consumption growth
in recent months. However, the very weak levels of

consumer and business sentiment could be pointing to
a more pronounced slowing in household and business
spending growth than assumed.
We have calibrated a downside domestic activity
scenario where there is a larger response in
household and business spending to these
headwinds. There is a range of estimates for the effects
of these headwinds in the literature, highlighting the
uncertainty around their impact on economic activity.
Drawing on this evidence, the scenario uses larger

responses than assumed in the central forecast, thereby
identifying a plausible downside outlook for domestic
activity. In the scenario, firms feel forced to pass on the
conflict-related costs to prices as in the central forecast;
but households respond more cautiously to the
resultant pressures on real income and wealth, reducing
discretionary consumption by more than assumed in the
central forecast. Such an effect on spending might be
compounded by a tightening in borrowing constraints

and incentives for housing investors. The scenario
assumes firms respond to weaker demand and margin
pressure with a sharper pullback on (non-data centre)
investment and hiring. Overall, the level of GDP is around
1.0 per cent lower than the central forecasts
by end-2028.
The larger pullback in household and business
spending results in weaker activity and employment
growth, but only modestly lower inflation than in
the central forecast, assuming interest rates and the

exchange rate follow the same path as the central
forecast. The greater caution exhibited by households
pushes the gross household saving ratio up gradually to
a peak of around 17 per cent, where it remains over the
remainder of the forecast period. The greater reduction
Chapter 3  |  OutlookOutlookOutlookOutlookOutlookOutlookOutlook
Reserve Bank of Australia  |  Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026 75

### Page 79

in hiring pushes the unemployment rate 0.3 percentage
points higher by end-2028, creating more spare capacity
in the domestic economy than in the central forecast
(Graph 3.12). The additional spare capacity in the
economy and the labour market reduces inflationary
pressures somewhat, but the assumption of cost
pass-through by firms limits this effect. Overall, trimmed
mean inflation reaches 21⁄2 per cent only one quarter
earlier than in the central forecast.

Key risk #4 – There remains
considerable uncertainty about the
degree of capacity pressures in the
domestic economy and labour
market.
Despite the recent reassessment, it is possible that

we are still understating the extent of capacity
pressures. While we assess that capacity constraints in
the economy and labour market have been tighter than
previously judged, model-based estimates of the
unemployment rate that is consistent with low and
stable inflation continue to point to greater tightness in
labour market conditions than our central assessment. In
addition, if productivity growth were to remain subdued
over the forecast period, rather than pick up modestly as

assumed, it may suggest that the economy’s supply
capacity would be lower than currently expected. The
implications for inflation would depend on how
households and businesses adjust their spending
and expectations.
Conversely, capacity pressures may be weaker than
expected. The broader suite of full employment
indicators points to somewhat less labour market
tightness than our central assessment. It is also possible
that more of the recent strength in wages growth and

inflation reflects factors other than excess demand, such
as conflict-related cost effects or higher inflation
expectations. In these cases, wages growth and inflation
may moderate more quickly than in the central forecast.
Productivity growth could also recover more quickly
than assumed, including if the anticipated benefits of AI
adoption and other technological developments are
realised sooner.
There is also a risk that the unemployment rate
increases by more than expected if labour supply

remains stronger than assumed. There is a risk that
elevated cost-of-living pressures could support labour
force participation for longer, or to a greater extent, than
assumed. If so, stronger labour supply could result in a
higher unemployment rate than forecast, even if labour
demand evolves broadly as expected.

### Page 1

AI 芯⽚公司 Cerebras 宣布签署微软云合作协议，六个⽉内完成亚⻢逊、甲⻣⽂、微软三家
超⼤规模云服务商合作布局，管理层重申 2027 年营收三倍增⻓⽬标。制造端产能年底将提
升逾 10 倍，且因不使⽤ HBM 可有效规避持续⾄ 2028 年的内存短缺及涨价压⼒，形成供应链
层⾯的结构性优势。
摘要：
AI 芯⽚初创公司 Cerebras 在 2026 年第三季度交出⾸次实现净利润转正的答卷，并宣布微软成为
其六个⽉内拿下的第三家超⼤规模云服务商客⼾，公司重申 2027 年营收将实现三倍增⻓的强劲
指引。
华尔街⻅闻提及，当地时间 11 ⽉ 12 ⽇，备受市场瞩⽬的 Cerebras 公布 2026 年第三季度财报。公
司当季核⼼营收达到 2.309 亿美元，同⽐增⻓ 70% ，⾼于分析师平均预期的 2.147 亿美元。
公司⾸次作为上市公司实现了⾮公认会计准则（ Non-GAAP ）下的净利润转正，达到 3000 万美
元。
随后电话会上， Cerebras 联合创始⼈兼 CEO Andrew Feldman 表⽰，公司同步上调第四季度及

全年指引，预计核⼼营收环⽐增速接近 40% 。公司还维持 2027 年营收 " 三倍增⻓ " 的⽬标，并预
期核⼼⽑利率持续向 60% 的⻓期⽬标靠拢。
Andrew Feldman 宣布，公司正式签署与微软的云合作协议，加上此前已公布的 AWS 和甲⻣⽂合
作， Cerebras 在过去六个⽉内完成三家超⼤规模云服务商的客件。他表⽰，微软协议的更多细
节将于明年上半年披露，相关收⼊亦预计于同期开始确认。
与此同时，⾸席财务官 Bob Komin 在电话会上披露，公司与 OpenAI 数据中⼼容量交付存在阶段
性滞后，已据此在剩余履约义务（ RPO ）中扣除相应延迟补偿额度。他同时强调，潜在取消量
占 OpenAI 合同总承诺容量不⾜ 5% ，公司预计于 2027 年下半年恢复正常交付进度。
制造端， Andrew Feldman 表⽰，公司正与 Flex 和 Sanmina 协同扩⼤产能，预计 2026 年底制造产
能将较年初提升逾 10 倍， 2027 年将进⼀步新增⼯⼚。
他还强调，由于公司不使⽤⾼带宽存储器（ HBM ），可有效规避持续⾄ 2028 年的内存短缺及涨

价压⼒，形成供应链层⾯的结构性优势。

拿下微软⼤单，六个⽉内集⻬三家 Hyperscaler
除了强劲的财务数据，市场最关注的增量信息是 Cerebras 在客⼾拓展上的重⼤突破。 Feldman
在会上正式宣布：
⾄此，加上此前宣布的 AWS 和甲⻣⽂， Cerebras 在短短六个⽉内已经赢得了三家超⼤规模云服
务商的⻘睐。这些协议将极⼤拓宽其市场渠道。
Feldman 透露，搭载 Cerebras 的甲⻣⽂云推理服务预计将于 2027 年夏天推出，⽽与微软合作的
更多细节将在明年初公布。
CFO Bob Komin 补充道，过去⼏个⽉⾥，公司的销售渠道增⻓了 “ 远超 10 倍 ” ，需求不仅来⾃超
⼤规模云服务商，还涵盖了主权云、企业客⼾和 AI 原⽣公司。
直⾯ OpenAI 合同争议： “ 延期是因为缺电和壳，不是缺芯⽚ ”
作为 Cerebras ⽬前最⼤的客⼾， OpenAI 的合同履⾏情况是投资者审视的焦点。截⾄ 9 ⽉ 30 ⽇，
Cerebras 的剩余履约义务（ RPO ）⾼达 261 亿美元，其中绝⼤部分来⾃ OpenAI 。

针对市场关于 OpenAI 订单交付延期的担忧，管理层给出了透明的回应。受制于全球数据中⼼建
设的普遍瓶颈， Cerebras 未能按原定激进的时间表交付基础设施。 CFO Bob Komin 坦⾔：
管理层已将潜在的延期违约⾦从 RPO 中扣除。 Bob Komin 强调，该违约⾦对营收和⽑利率的影
响相对有限，原因在于违约⾦额占 RPO ⽐例较⼩，且将在未来数年内摊销确认。公司预计在
2027 年下半年能赶上进度。
Feldman 更是直⾔不讳地指出，瓶颈完全不在于硅⽚或系统制造：
他强调，不仅是 Cerebras ，包括 OpenAI 在内的所有⾏业巨头的数据中⼼建设 “ 都在全⾯延期 ” 。
同时， Feldman 坚决辟谣了关于 Cerebras 技术⽆法⽀持 OpenAI ⼤模型的传⾔：
打破 “ 低良率 ” 偏⻅，不依赖 HBM 成战略优势
今天，我们与微软签署了⼀项重要的云协议。
如果延期达到 90 天或以上，双⽅都有权取消该部分容量。但我们估计，今年可能⾯临取消的容
量将不到总承诺兆⽡数的 5% 。

我们有产能，我们有所有材料。我们等待的是⼟地、电⼒和外壳（ Land, Power, Shell ）的完
⼯。
有谣⾔称 Cerebras 因技术原因⽆法⽀持 Astra 。这些都是假的。我们的系统完全能够⽀持 Astra
规模甚⾄更⼤的模型。

⾯对华尔街对晶圆级计算（ Wafer-Scale Computing ）架构良率的质疑， Feldman ⽤了⼀个⽐喻
来进⾏反击：
他明确表⽰，⽆论是台积电的代⼯⼚良率还是参数良率， “ 我们的良率都⾮常⾼。 ”

带宽内存（ HBM ），这使其在全球供应链中占据了独特优势。 Feldman 补充称：
Andrew Feldman 在电话会上介绍，公司⽬前已签约约 700 兆⽡总数据中⼼容量，计划于 2027 年
底前完成交付。超过 75% 的新增容量将落地美国境外，近期将有加拿⼤、法国和挪威的新设施
陆续投⼊运营，同期还在挪威和芬兰追加签约，并处于在圣保罗签约南美⾸个数据中⼼的最终

吉⽡级容量谈判。

WSE-5 芯⽚已回⽚，明年夏天量产
在技术迭代上， Cerebras 继续保持激进的步伐。
公司宣布，其下⼀代处理器 Wafer-Scale Engine 5 （ WSE-5 ）已经从代⼯⼚回⽚，进度超前。
Feldman ⾃豪地表⽰：
基于台积电 5 纳⽶⼯艺的 CS-5 系统将在 2027 年初夏投⼊⽣产，性能将⽐ CS-4 翻倍。
此外，公司还率先推出了异构解耦推理（ DISAG ）⽅案，将 AMD 的 GPU ⽤于预填充（ Pre-fill ），
将 Cerebras 的 CS-3 系统⽤于解码，⼤幅提⾼了每兆⽡的算⼒产出和利润率。
针对市场上关于低批次 GPU 可挑战 Cerebras 推理速度的讨论， Andrew Feldman 在问答环节表
⽰， GPU 通过降低批次规模可换取⼀定速度提升，但代价是单位 Token 成本⼤幅上升。
他指出，在每秒约 250 ⾄ 300 个 Token 的速度区间以上， Cerebras 的经济性已明显占优；在每秒
800 ⾄ 2000 个 Token 区间， GPU 在技术上已⽆法达到同等性能。

Cerebras Q3 财报电话会全⽂（ AI 辅助翻译）：
Cerebras 发明了⼀种全新的计算架构。⽤为其他架构设计的指标和思维模型来套⽤我们，就像
检查⼀辆特斯拉，然后问：排⽓管在哪⾥？电动汽⻋需要不同的参照系，晶圆级计算也是如此。
我们认为，内存短缺及其相关的价格上涨将持续到 2028 年，这将推⾼竞争对⼿的成本并限制其
供应。全⾏业的内存挑战，对 Cerebras 来说正是机会。
WSE-5 于 6 ⽉ 15 ⽇流⽚。芯⽚现已返回，已点亮，并已经在运⾏模型，正在进⾏机箱内测试。在
不到五个⽉的时间内从流⽚进⼊系统测试，这是⾮凡的执⾏⼒。

下⾯，我将会议移交给 Andrew 。
Andrew Feldman （⾸席执⾏官兼联合创始⼈）：谢谢 Sean ，也感谢⼤家今天的参与。
展望未来数⽉，我们看到两个确定性趋势：快速变化与算⼒短缺。过去 90 天⾥， AI ⽣态系统⻅
证了前沿模型领导者数度易主，数⼗款有价值的开源模型相继发布，重⼤安全事件相继发⽣，强
⼤的新型消费者智能体不断涌现，还有许多其他发展动态。
任何⼈都⽆法预测 90 天后 AI 的能⼒⾛向，更遑论⼀年之后。然⽽，在所有这些变化中，智能与
速度始终是每⼀个应⽤场景的两⼤核⼼要素 —— ⽆论是编程、安全，还是个⼈智能体。

在这个快速变化的世界中， Cerebras 的使命是确保⽆论 AI 如何发展， Cerebras 都能持续增⻓。
基础模型增⻓，我们增⻓；开源模型增⻓，我们增⻓；个⼈智能体增⻓，我们增⻓；⽆论是企业
还是初创公司增⻓，我们增⻓；⽆论客⼾需要硬件还是云计算资源，我们都能增⻓。我们的⽬标
是，⽆论什么因素驱动⾏业发展， Cerebras 都能赢得先机。
第三季度，我们在这⼀⽬标上取得了显著进展。
⾸先，我们超越了所有业绩指引⽬标。在对这些成绩感到满意的同时，我认为更重要的是 —— ⾃
上次财报电话会议以来，我们与多家 AI ⾏业领军企业达成了重要合作，最新进展是今天宣布与微
软签署了⼀项重要云服务协议。加上此前宣布的 AWS 和甲⻣⽂合作，迄今已有三家超⼤规模云
服务商选择将 Cerebras 平台纳⼊其基础架构战略。
这些新合作，叠加现有客⼾的持续增⻓，使我们得以全⾯上调第四季度核⼼业绩指引，并继续预
期 2027 年营收实现三倍增⻓，同时⽑利率持续扩张。 Bob 将在其发⾔部分详细介绍第三季度表

现及前瞻性指引。
关于业务结构

### Page 5

作为⼀家 AI 加速器公司，我们的核⼼创新在于芯⽚、系统和软件，使 AI 计算更快速。我们是⼀家
AI 硬件公司，拥有两条市场渠道。
第⼀条渠道是系统销售：将 Cerebras 硬件机架安装在客⼾的数据中⼼。这是⼀项优质业务，收
⼊确认快、回款快，但也具有⼀定的不规律性，取决于客⼾数据中⼼是否具备接收硬件的能⼒。
第⼆条渠道是我们的云服务。 AI 原⽣企业这⼀快速增⻓的客群，更倾向于消费算⼒（ token ），⽽
⾮⾃⾏部署硬件。鉴于数据中⼼和算⼒资源极为稀缺，我们同时拥有系统和数据中⼼，构成了重
要的战略优势。
对于⾃主⽣产硬件的公司⽽⾔， AI 云业务尤为出⾊。我们每兆⽡的成本远低于新兴云服务商，因
此能够实现更⾼的资本效率。两条渠道的业务⽐例会因季度⽽异，但我们相信两者都将随时间⼤
幅增⻓。
关于与 OpenAI 合作的市场猜测
OpenAI 是我们的重要⼤客⼾，也是紧密合作伙伴，我相信未来双⽅将继续保持这⼀关系。
OpenAI 在第三季度相较第⼆季度实现了增⻓，我们预计未来还将进⼀步增⻓。

OpenAI 基于 Cerebras 推出的 UltraFast SOL5-6 预览版取得了压倒性的成功，有⼒证明了市场对
快速 token 的需求极为巨⼤。
与此同时， Cerebras 确实没有⾜够的算⼒⽀持 UltraFast 在包括 Apsara 在内的更⼤范围内推出。
正如业界所述，我们认为算⼒是⾏业商业决策的关键驱动因素。事实上，即使 OpenAI 拥有海量
GPU 供应，仍需实施基于使⽤量的限制措施。
协议签署后，我们花费的时间⽐预期更⻓，才找到合同并上线满⾜协议要求所需的⼤量数据中⼼
增量产能。因此，我们⽬前在初始阶段的执⾏上有所滞后。
鉴于这⼀合作对我们业务的重要性，我们理解投资者对其给予⾼度关注。关于协议细节或相关新
闻，信息来源不太可能来⾃ Cerebras 或 OpenAI 以外的渠道。
例如，此前有传⾔称 Cerebras 出于技术原因⽆法⽀持 Astra ，这是错误的。 CS3 、 CS4 和 CS5 完
全有能⼒⽀持 Astra 级别的模型，乃⾄更⼤规模的模型。

同时，我预期并建议投资者预期， OpenAI 将利⽤多种合作关系和多样化硬件⽅案构建算⼒。
OpenAI 将淘汰旧模型，在这些计算资源上部署新模型，⽤于内部和外部⽤途。他们的算⼒需求
极为庞⼤，不同解决⽅案在其规模化进程中均有机会发挥作⽤。
关于推理速度的竞争地位
在此我想补充⼀点：尽管市场上存在各种实验性⽅案 —— 低批次 GPU 、初创公司发布、超⼤型
计算核⼼、超⼤规模云服务商的内部定制芯⽚ —— 我们依然是毫⽆争议的最快推理解决⽅案。我
们相信，我们的产品路线图将使我们在未来数年内保持这⼀优势。
业务评估框架：客⼾、算⼒、能⼒
我们对业务进展的评估框架保持不变，围绕三个维度进⾏：客⼾、算⼒和能⼒。
客⼾
我们正在快速拓展客⼾群，以扩⼤覆盖范围、多元化收⼊来源。第三季度是成果丰硕的⼀个季
度。我们赢得了两家新超⼤规模云服务商的合作。

### Page 6

我们与甲⻣⽂签署协议，将 Cerebras 硬件引⼊甲⻣⽂云， Cerebras 将部署在甲⻣⽂数据中⼼，
服务于 AI 实验室、企业客⼾以及构建交互式智能应⽤的开发者。我们预计由 Cerebras 驱动的甲
⻣⽂云推理服务将于 2027 年夏季正式上线。
我们还与微软签署了令⼈振奋的合作协议，相关详情将于明年进⼀步披露。
⾄此，我们在过去六个⽉内已赢得三家超⼤规模云服务商的合作，这是重要的⾥程碑成就，为我
们 2027 年和 2028 年的发展奠定了良好基础。
我们还与多家⾼质量新兴云服务商扩⼤了合作，包括 Firework 等，进⼀步多元化我们的市场渠
道，为客⼾提供更多访问 Cerebras ⾼速推理服务的途径。
开源权重模型同样为我们带来了助⼒，这些模型持续催⽣新应⽤，拓展 AI 在⽇常⽣活和各⾏业中
的应⽤边界。例如，就在本周，我们宣布 Cerebras 快速推理服务正在为 Built 公司的 OS 平台及个
⼈助⼿提供⽀持，为数百万⽤⼾带来极速推理体验。
通过 Built 这样的消费者应⽤，我们将 Cerebras 的强⼤算⼒直接送到⽤⼾⼿中。这正是快速 token

能够获得溢价的原因 —— 我们的云客⼾告诉我们，快速 token 能产⽣出⾊的⽑利率，⽽ Cerebras
的快速 token 是他们销售的利润最⾼的 token 。
算⼒
算⼒包含两个组成部分：数据中⼼算⼒和制造产能，两者对于业务增⻓都⾄关重要。
数据中⼼可⽤性是整个⾏业⾯临的持续性制约，我们也不例外。此外，我们认为美国政策环境的
演变可能会为美国境内的数据中⼼建设带来更⼤挑战。
2027 年，我们超过 75% 的数据中⼼产能将在美国境外上线。实际上，未来⼏个⽉内，加拿⼤、
法国和挪威的新设施将陆续投⼊运营。本季度，我们还签署了挪威和芬兰新增产能的合同，进⼀
步扩⼤了欧洲业务布局。同时，我们正处于在圣保罗签约数据中⼼的最后阶段，这将是我们在南
美洲的⾸个数据中⼼。
迄今为⽌，我们已为 2027 年底前交付签署了约 700 兆⽡的数据中⼼产能合同。我们的⾃有云已跻
⾝⾮超⼤规模云服务商中规模较⼤之列，⽬前正深⼊推进吉⽡级产能的谈判。
提升数据中⼼产能的最佳途径，是提升数据中⼼硬件的 token ⽣成能⼒，即每兆⽡产⽣更多

token。为此，分解式推理（ disaggregated inference ）通过将数据中⼼的 token 交付能⼒提升
最多 5 倍，有效扩⼤了我们的产能。每兆⽡产⽣更多超快 token ，意味着每个数据中⼼创造的收
⼊将显著增加。
在制造和供应链⽅⾯，我们正与 Flex 和 Sanmina 协⼒扩⼤制造规模，预计 2026 年底的制造产能
将超过年初的 10 倍。 2027 年，我们计划增设⼯⼚、进⼀步提升产能，以⽀撑预期的⼤幅增⻓。
我们的供应链也已做好⽀持这⼀增⻓的充分准备。
最重要的是，我们已获得台积电充⾜的晶圆产能承诺，台积电持续彰显其世界级合作伙伴的实
⼒。
同样值得关注的是我们供应链所不需要的东西 —— 这对我们同样⾄关重要。与其他竞争对⼿不
同，我们不使⽤ HBM 内存。我们认为，内存短缺及由此引发的价格上涨将持续⾄ 2028 年，这将
推⾼竞争对⼿的成本并限制其供应。⾏业性内存挑战为 Cerebras 创造了机遇。
⼯⼚和供应是扩⼤产能的必要条件，但并不充分，我们还必须保持⾼良率。

### Page 7

关于良率问题
Cerebras 发明了⼀种全新的计算架构。⽤针对其他架构设计的指标和思维模型来衡量我们，就
如同检查⼀辆特斯拉时追问排⽓管在哪⾥。晶圆级计算需要不同的参照框架，正如电动汽⻋需要
不同的评估维度⼀样。
我们欢迎就设计的各个⽅⾯提出问题，并将努⼒更清晰地分享架构细节。
然⽽，⾦融界存在⼀些持续流传的不实之词：⼀是声称我们的系统⽆法⽀持⼤型模型 —— 这显然
是错误的，因为我们⽬前已在运⾏⼤型模型；⼆是声称我们的制造良率低。两者均不属实。
关于模型规模问题，我已在前⾯的发⾔及此前的讨论中做出了回应。由于制造良率较难从外部观
察，我将在此直接加以说明。
良率分为两类：晶圆⼚良率和参数良率。我们来⾃台积电的晶圆⼚良率很⾼，参数良率同样如
此。我们的技术博客对此有详细介绍，以下做简要说明。
所有晶圆都含有缺陷。⼏⼗年来，这些缺陷是业界认为晶圆级处理器不可能实现的核⼼原因。我
们通过冗余设计解决了这⼀问题。
我们的设计包含约⼀百万个完全相同的计算单元（ tile ），其中⼀⼩部分作为备⽤。当缺陷导致某

个单元失效时，硬件会⾃动绕过它并激活备⽤单元。这⼀机制在晶圆⼚⽣产阶段和现场使⽤阶段
均有效，实现了⾼晶圆良率和强⼤的现场可靠性。
第⼆个挑战是参数变异（ parametric variation ）。即使硅功能正常，其电⽓特性也会在晶圆各处
存在差异 —— 在相同电压下，某些区域运⾏速度更快或更慢。这是芯⽚设计的基本现实，也是实
现晶圆级计算必须克服的障碍之⼀。
我们通过三种⽅式解决参数变异问题：
第⼀，  去除晶圆外缘。外缘是晶圆最薄弱的部分，也是参数变异的主要来源。我们使⽤适合 300
毫⽶晶圆的最⼤正⽅形区域，剔除⼤量边缘硅⽚。
第⼆，  针对宽泛的⼯况进⾏设计和测试。我们的核⼼能够容忍更⼤范围的变异。在时序收敛
（即验证电路满⾜时序要求的过程）阶段，我们在远⽐⾏业常规更宽泛的⼯作条件下对设计进⾏
验证，从⽽确保在晶圆各处变异的情况下仍能实现预期性能。
第三，  在晶圆各区域独⽴调节电压。我们发明了⼀种技术，可在晶圆的 84 个分区内独⽴调节核
⼼电压 —— 对较慢区域提⾼电压，对较快区域降低电压，从⽽对性能进⾏精确控制。据我们所

知，⽬前没有其他芯⽚具备这⼀能⼒。
最终结果是在晶圆⼚和⼯⼚两个环节均实现了极⾼的良率。我们通过⼯程化⼿段解决了其他⼈认
为使晶圆级计算不可能实现的问题，从⽽达成了这⼀成就。
最后补充⼀点：有⼈将我们硬件出货的相对较低⽑利率视为良率问题的证据，⽽实际上这仅仅反
映了我们制造能⼒的持续提升过程，以及在早期客⼾特殊定价协议下的最终批次出货情况。我们
预计，未来⼏个季度硬件⽑利率将⼤幅改善，最终超越 50% 的⻓期⽬标。
能⼒
我们在各个⽅⾯持续提升能⼒：新系统与处理器、改进的软件、先进的制造技术，以及提供⾏业
最佳解决⽅案的合作伙伴关系。

### Page 8

尽管 AI ⾏业变化节奏极快，推理速度的重要性仍在持续提升， Cerebras 依然是⽆可争议的领导
者。我们相信这⼀性能优势将⻓期持续，为此我们的产品路线图持续快速推进。
CS4 即将进⼊全⾯量产，性能和吞吐量均将翻倍。
我们的下⼀代处理器 —— 晶圆级引擎 5 （ WSE-5 ），已部署于第五代 CS5 系统，已从晶圆⼚如期
归来，甚⾄早于计划。 WSE-5 于 6 ⽉ 15 ⽇完成流⽚，芯⽚已回⼚启动，正在运⾏模型，并已进⼊
整机测试阶段。从流⽚到系统测试不⾜五个⽉，是出⾊的执⾏⼒体现。 CS5 的速度将⽐ CS4 提升
⼀倍以上，预计于 2027 年初夏进⼊量产。 CS5 基于台积电 5 纳⽶⼯艺节点，该节点的晶圆竞争相
对不那么激烈，晶圆成本也⼤幅降低。
我们的创新还延伸⾄完整解决⽅案领域。据我们所知， Cerebras 是⾸家实现⽣产级异构分解式
推理的公司。⽬前，我们的云服务正在使⽤ AMD GPU 处理预填充（ pre-fill ），使⽤ Cerebras
CS3 系统处理解码（ decode ）。为⽀持⽣产级上线，我们构建了⼀套分解式推理软件平台，兼容

AMD 及其他 GPU 产品，并将在 Cerebras 推理云及部分数据中⼼客⼾中部署同款⽅案。
分解式推理在相同功耗下能够提供更多⾼速 token ，这意味着云服务商每兆⽡获得更⾼利润， AI
⽤⼾获得更低的每 token 成本。我们相信分解式推理将成为主流云部署的默认配置之⼀。
我们的软件栈持续成熟，云服务实现了令⼈印象深刻的⾼可⽤性，系统运⾏速度不断提升，新模
型的启动时间也已缩短⾄仅需⼏天。
Cerebras 是 Kimi K3.1 的零⽇上线合作伙伴 ——Kimi K3.1 是⽉之暗⾯旗下的 2.8 万亿参数前沿级
开源权重模型，或许是⽬前市场上最顶尖的开源权重模型。 Cerebras 上运⾏的 Kimi K3.1 不仅是
业界最快，运⾏速度⾼达每秒 2000 个 token 。
⾛过近 11 年的历程，我们继续以惊⼈的速度创新。我们的团队在顺境与逆境中始终凝聚在⼀
起，公司内部的活⼒与激情从未如此⾼涨。
我们为解决⾏业中最艰难的问题⽽感到⾃豪。
总结：  我们超越所有指标，全⾯上调第四季度指引，并预期 2027 年营收三倍增⻓。我们在各核

⼼领域取得重要进展，包括新增两家超⼤规模云服务商客⼾，在过去六个⽉内已累计赢得三家超
⼤规模云服务商。 CS4 已开始出货，第五代处理器 WSE-5 已回⼚，并以创纪录的速度完成 CS5
整机测试。 AMD ⽣产级分解式推理已上线，对另⼀款 2.8 万亿参数模型的零⽇⽀持也以⾏业最佳
性能成功实现。

Bob Komin （⾼级副总裁兼⾸席财务官）：谢谢 Andrew ，⼤家下午好。在介绍第三季度业绩和
第四季度展望的具体内容之前，我想先回顾⼀下我们的业务规模化历程。
业务战略背景
我们选择在 2025 年初启动云服务，原因在于在这个快速演进的 AI 格局中， AI 原⽣企业希望快速
扩展算⼒，⽽不愿⾃建数据中⼼。由于超⼤规模云服务商会提前数年规划其数据中⼼算⼒分配，
往往⽆法为新硬件提供近期的⾃有设施空间。为了赢得基础模型实验室和超⼤规模云服务商所需
的⼤型部署合同， Cerebras 需要证明我们的系统能够在规模化场景下提供⾏业领先的推理性
能。在私有云中充分评估我们的解决⽅案之后，这些客⼾可以选择购买硬件，或采购我们的云服

务。
为了尽快提供⾜够的云算⼒以满⾜这些需求，我们选择尽快租⽤数据中⼼产能，将私有云规模向
吉⽡级推进。这⼀总体战略被证明是正确的 —— 数据中⼼产能⽬前已极为稀缺，⽽我们已有⼤量

### Page 9

产能在合同中锁定。
更重要的是，拥有私有云之后，我们得以向整个市场展⽰快速推理的价值，并进⼀步激发市场需
求。由于新型⼤型数据中⼼从⽴项到投⼊使⽤需要 15 ⾄ 18 个⽉，为了加快满⾜我们已有的⼤量
需求，我们选择从⼀家客⼾处回租了部分⾃有设备。如前所述，这在短期内对⽑利率造成了下⾏
压⼒，但也使我们能够在客⼾有需求时及时服务，从⽽拓展现有客⼾、赢得重要新客⼾。
如今，我们已赢得 OpenAI 等前沿实验室，以及 AWS 等超⼤规模云服务商，近期⼜新增了两家超
⼤规模云服务商客⼾。 Cognition 、 Figma 、 Block 、 Built 等领先客⼾也已加⼊我们的云平台。我
们以客⼾需求为导向，因地制宜 —— 有时销售硬件，有时提供云服务。
此前，我们也已说明， 2026 年将是⼀个建设之年 —— 扩⼤算⼒、提升能⼒、积累客⼾，为 2027
年营收实现三倍以上增⻓奠定基础。凭借今年迄今取得的强劲进展，我们有充分信⼼实现这⼀⽬
标。
第三季度业绩
现在转⼊第三季度业绩，我很⾼兴地宣布，第四季度将是我们增⻓和核⼼财务趋势的重要拐点。

我们已连续第三个季度超越核⼼财务指引。我们还提前实现了⾸次⾮ GAAP 净利润转正，早于预
期。
提⽰⼀下，核⼼指标的定义及其与 GAAP 的调节表已包含在今⽇财报发布中，并可在我们的⽹站
上查阅。
核⼼营收：  创历史新⾼，达 2.31 亿美元，环⽐增⻓ 10% ，同⽐增⻓ 70% ，超出我们 2.15 亿美元
指引中点 1600 万美元，超出幅度为 7% 。
核⼼云服务及其他服务营收：  同样创下历史新⾼，同⽐增⻓ 272% ，达 1.45 亿美元。第三季度云
服务营收环⽐增⻓ 13% 。我们⽬前预计第四季度云服务营收将基本持平。这⼀趋势并⾮由定价或
需求问题驱动 —— 定价环境依然健康，我们解决⽅案的需求持续远超供给。
云服务营收的增⻓瓶颈完全在于数据中⼼产能。我们将于 12 ⽉开始提升数据中⼼产能，为明年
积蓄动能，但遗憾的是时间太晚，⽆法对第四季度产⽣实质影响。我们预计 2027 年数据中⼼产
能将出现更为可观的增加，主要增量将从明年年中开始释放，届时我们将交付⽬前已在合同中锁

定的 700 兆⽡数据中⼼产能的较⼤部分。
核⼼硬件营收：  本季度为 8600 万美元，环⽐增⻓ 5% 。我们看到客⼾对快速推理服务的需求不断
增⻓，不仅来⾃我们的私有云，还来⾃与多家新客⼾签署的硬件采购合同。随着这些硬件发货计
划于第四季度启动，核⼼总营收将出现⼤幅跃升。
第三季度⾄第四季度的核⼼营收环⽐增⻓将接近 40% ，同⽐增⻓约为 85% 。这些硬件订单（包括
甲⻣⽂订单）将⾄少持续发货⾄ 2027 年上半年。
正如 Andrew 前⾯提到的，我们今天还宣布微软成为新客⼾。过去⼏个⽉，我们的销售管线规模
增⻓超过 10 倍，驱动因素涵盖多元化机遇，包括超⼤规模云服务商、新兴云服务商、主权云、
企业客⼾和 AI 原⽣公司等各类新⽼客⼾的需求。
⽑利率
第三季度核⼼⽑利率为 41% ，较第⼆季度环⽐改善 40 个基点，并⽐我们的季度指引⾼出 200 个基
点。

### Page 10

除了回租系统对⽑利率的影响外，本季度我们还⾯临⼀些逆⻛因素 —— 来⾃多年前签署的硬件采
购协议（采⽤较低定价）的最后⼀批发货正在消化。我们之前已指引，所有这些因素将造成暂时
性⽑利率下滑，低点将出现在第三季度。实际上，我们的表现优于预期 —— 低点已于上季度（第
⼆季度）出现。
现将第三季度核⼼⽑利率分拆如下：
核⼼硬件⽑利率  环⽐下降⾄ 31% ，主要由年前客⼾协议项下最后批次发货所致。我们⽬前正以
⼤幅提升后的现⾏价格执⾏硬件订单，这也意味着从第四季度起⽑利率将显著改善。
核⼼云服务及其他服务⽑利率  ⼤幅提升⾄ 46.9% ，较第⼆季度提升 510 个基点。主要驱动因素是
以更⾼定价和利润率承接的新业务。部分被从客⼾处回租数据中⼼产能和硬件的较⾼成本所抵
消。我们预计这⼀情况将⾄少持续⾄ 2027 年年中，直⾄我们拥有⾜够的⾃有数据中⼼产能来满
⾜较⾼⽔平的云需求。
综合以上情况，第四季度核⼼⽑利率预计将实现阶梯式改善，提升 700 个基点，⾄中点值 48% 。
我们预计核⼼总⽑利率将在近期超过 50% ，并持续向 60% 的⻓期⽬标推进。

盈利能⼒进展
第三季度及年初⾄今，我们在盈利能⼒⽅⾯取得了强劲进展。核⼼经营利润率环⽐改善 170 个基
点，亏损率降⾄ 14.3% ，超出指引中点近 10 个百分点。核⼼经营亏损与第⼆季度持平，约为
3300 万美元。核⼼净利润转正，达 3000 万美元。年初⾄今调整后核⼼ EBITDA 也实现正值，为
200 万美元。
随着第四季度营收和⽑利率⼤幅增⻓，我们预计所有核⼼关键盈利指标（包括经营利润率）将于
下季度转为正值。
剩余履约义务（ RPO ）与 OpenAI 合同
截⾄ 9 ⽉ 30 ⽇，剩余履约义务增⾄ 261 亿美元。我们按季度更新 RPO ，纳⼊新签业务，并根据剩
余承诺期内预计待确认⾦额的变化对现有合同价值进⾏调整。第三季度 RPO 不含 9 ⽉ 30 ⽇之后签
署的新合同影响，包括甲⻣⽂这⼀⼤额合同。
关于 OpenAI 合同的状态，投资者提问颇多，因为其占我们迄今已报告 RPO 的⼤部分。提⽰⼀
下，该合同已在 IPO 前随 S1 ⽂件提交，是⼀份 750 兆⽡算⼒的照付不议（ take-or-pay ）合同。

合同包含众多对双⽅均适⽤的条款，其中有⼀条规定：若我们在交付算⼒⽅⾯出现延误，
OpenAI 有权在此期间获得抵扣额度，⽤于未来的承诺采购。我们已计算潜在延误抵扣额度，并
已在 9 ⽉ 30 ⽇的 RPO 中予以扣除。
这些抵扣对营收和⽑利率的影响较⼩，原因有⼆：⼀是占 RPO 的⽐例相对较⼩；⼆是绝⼤部分
营收将在未来数年内按⽐例确认，抵扣⾦额将随之分摊。未来每个季度将根据最新估算进⾏更
新，并在必要时反映在指引中。
若算⼒交付延误持续达 90 天或以上， OpenAI 和 Cerebras 双⽅均有权取消该部分算⼒承诺（仅限
该部分）。我们估计今年可能⾯临取消⻛险的算⼒量，将低于总承诺兆⽡数的 5% 。
鉴于全球数据中⼼产能需求旺盛，我们在寻找并上线初始数据中⼼产能以满⾜交付计划⽅⾯，相
较极为紧张的时间表花费了额外时间。根据我们团队对各站点的频繁实地检查，我们⽬前预计将
于 2027 年下半年追回进度，届时未来延误抵扣⻛险应会相应降低。

### Page 11

我们采取了保守态度，未假设任何延误将因超出我们控制范围的外部依赖因素⽽被豁免。若豁免
情况发⽣，将减少我们所预估的延误抵扣⾦额。
此外，即便 OpenAI 或 Cerebras 选择取消某批次延误算⼒，我们认为凭借充⾜的市场需求，完全
可以找到其他客⼾承接。
OpenAI 还保留了在初始 750 兆⽡承诺之外，另⾏采购最多 1.25 吉⽡算⼒的选择权，最早可于
2029 年起上线。鉴于这还有三年时间，且 AI 领域变化之快，我们尚未就此展开讨论。
资产负债表与资本效率
我们继续保持强劲的流动性。第三季度末，现⾦、现⾦等价物、受限现⾦及有价证券合计约 78
亿美元。我们还拥有最⾼ 8.5 亿美元的循环信贷额度，⽬前尚未动⽤。
今年 1 ⽉获得的 10 亿美元预付流动资⾦贷款，以及作为 OpenAI 合同部分内容⽤于新数据中⼼产
能装修的⾮经常性直通费⽤的持续收款，已为今年迄今⼤部分净营运资本需求和资本⽀出提供了
资⾦。
我们不是⼀家新兴云服务商，⽽是业界最快推理解决⽅案的垂直整合发明者。我们的业务具备技

术差异化所赋予的韧性，我们相信这将在⻓期内驱动更⾼的利润潜⼒和现⾦创造能⼒。
我们的模式在已部署算⼒设备的资本投⼊⽅⾯也较低。对于我们在数据中⼼⾃主制造的⼤量设备
—— 与其他选择⾃研并部署 AI 芯⽚的公司类似 —— 我们⽆需向第三⽅⽀付 70% ⽑利率，也⽆需使
⽤⾼价 HBM ，只需⽀付我们的物料清单（ BOM ）成本。这既降低了每兆⽡的相对资本⽀出，⼜
使我们能够⽣产最快速、最有价值的 token 。
这反映在我们的资本结构中 —— 尽管我们私有云建设规模快速扩张，资产负债表上唯⼀的债务仅
为 OpenAI 流动资⾦贷款。我们还持续从客⼾处获得预付款，这是提升资本效率的另⼀杠杆。
我们当前的资本结构包含充裕的现⾦和投资，负债极少，为我们以多种⽅式持续融资、⽀撑快速
增⻓提供了充分灵活性 —— 这在当前资本市场环境下尤为有利。
展望

核⼼营收： 9.40 亿⾄ 9.60 亿美元

总结

### Page 12

第三季度是 Cerebras ⼜⼀创纪录的季度，第四季度亦将如此。我们灵活的硬件与云服务兼售模
式正在推动营收强劲增⻓，⽑利率改善也⽐预期提前了整整⼀个季度。
我们在制造和数据中⼼产能规模化⽅⾯取得了显著进展，这将使我们能够在 2027 年实现营收三
倍增⻓，同时核⼼⽑利率向 60% 的⻓期⽬标持续推进。我们已展现出强劲的运营杠杆效应，并预
计第四季度核⼼经营利润将基本实现盈亏平衡，此后预计在 2027 年⼤幅向 40% 的⻓期经营利润
率⽬标迈进。
这种规模、增⻓与盈利能⼒的综合优势，使我们成为独具特⾊的领先 AI 系统提供商。
最后补充⼀点：提醒⼤家，我们上市已逾 180 天， IPO 股票锁定期已于 11 ⽉ 9 ⽇全部届满解锁。

### Page 13

Andrew Feldman ：
这是个⾮常好的问题。 Profit 公司在其 S1 或某条推⽂中提到，他们只⽤了约三天时间完成了 AMD
GPU 的迁移上线。
这是⼀个⾮常有意义且重要的进展，尤其对于⻓期视⻆的观察者⽽⾔。我认为，业界尚未充分认
识到这对我们⽣态系统意味着什么。
编译器和底层软件历来是新架构⾯临的根本障碍，它们天然有利于在位者，也是⼈们常说的 " 软
件护城河 " 的核⼼来源。如果 Profit 公司真能实现他们所说的，那就意味着底层软件的价值将收
缩，并随时间趋近于消失。软件护城河也将随之收窄并消弭。
若底层软件的价值趋于零，真正能够提供价值的，就只剩下创新硬件。这意味着竞争分化将更多
取决于硬件的差异化程度。
我认为，这对 Cerebras 这样的创新硬件公司 —— 以及其他采⽤全新架构的硬件先⾏者 —— 是巨
⼤的优势，同时将削弱在位者⻓期以来享有的优势地位。
这个问题问得很好，值得整个⾏业密切关注。谢谢。

Quinn Bolton （ Needham ）：
⼤家好，祝贺取得进展。 Andrew ，我想确认⼀下我理解是否正确：在 RPO 和您的假设中，您已

Andrew Feldman：正确。
Quinn Bolton：
好的，很好。第⼆个问题：市场上有⼀种担忧，认为低批次 GPU 能够实现快速推理。听起来那
是⼀种成本很⾼的 token 交付⽅式。能否⽐较⼀下 Cerebras 与低批次 GPU 配置的每 token 成本？
Andrew Feldman ：

### Page 15

当然。 GPU 可以通过降低批次规模来换取更⾼的速度，但代价是牺牲吞吐量 —— 低批次下吞吐
量很低，⽽当你试图提⾼速度时， GPU 会进⼊低批次区间，总吞吐量变得⾮常⼩。
这是由架构特性决定的。可以这样理解： GPU 通过降批次确实能获得⼀定程度的性能提升，但
这样做时，每 token 成本会急剧上升 —— 同时服务的⽤⼾数⼤幅减少，总 token ⽣产量极为有限。
随着速度要求进⼀步提⾼，我们的优势会越来越强。当速度达到 GPU ⽆论如何都⽆法提供的⽔
平时， Cerebras 是唯⼀的选项 —— 在批次为 1 的情况下， GPU 仍然⽆法与我们媲美。
关于成本交叉点：⼤约在每秒 250 ⾄ 300 个 token 时，我们在经济性上更有优势；到每秒 500 、
800 、 1000 个 token 时，我们的优势会远超对⼿；在 800 ⾄ 2000 个 token 的区间， GPU 根本⽆法参
与竞争。这就是当前竞争格局的基本⾯貌。这个问题涉及复杂话题，提得很好。
Quinn Bolton：谢谢， Andrew 。

接线员：感谢提问。下⼀个问题来⾃ TD Cowen 的 Joshua Bruckheimer 。请提问。
Joshua Bruckheimer （ TD Cowen ）：
⼤家好，感谢接受提问，并祝贺微软合作顺利落地。⾸先，请谈谈在⼟地、电⼒和建筑问题的背
景下，您是如何分配算⼒的？看起来硅⽚和机架供应不是问题，那么在增量硬件销售⽅⾯还有多
⼤ 空 间 ？ 另 外 ， 鉴 于 云 客 ⼾ ⾯ 临 ⼟ 地 、 电 ⼒ 和 建 筑 ⽅ ⾯ 的 挑 战 ， 是 否 与 OpenAI 讨 论 过 由
OpenAI 或其合作伙伴购买硬件并部署在他们⾃⼰的数据中⼼？谢谢。
Andrew Feldman：这个问题确实有两层。 Josh ，第⼀层是：⼟地、电⼒和建筑及其施⼯是否
真的是限制因素？答案是肯定的。台积电已向我们做出了 2027 年充⾜晶圆产能的承诺，我们对
此⾮常有把握。不使⽤ HBM 显然也是极⼤的优势，因为 HBM ⽬前供应极为紧张。
你第⼆层问题问得很好：这是否为我们在数据中⼼建设期间销售硬件创造了机会？唯⼀需要注意

的是，据我们所知，⼏乎所有⼈的数据中⼼建设都在延期 —— 包括 OpenAI ，包括我们知道的所
有超⼤规模云服务商数据中⼼。数据中⼼延期是全球性的普遍现象，并⾮我们独有，我们的客⼾
同样⾯临这⼀制约。所以，答案是：是的，我们有产品供应；是的，我们正在向拥有数据中⼼的
客⼾发货；但同样，当前数据中⼼普遍延期，确实影响了实际进展。是否清楚了？
Joshua Bruckheimer ：
明⽩，但三问中的最后⼀个，也是我提的问题⾥⽐较难听的⼀部分 ——OpenAI 合同是否有可能
转为硬件采购？
Andrew Feldman ：
抱歉，合同中确实赋予了他们这⼀权利，但据我们了解，他们⾃⼰的数据中⼼建设同样落后于计
划。
Joshua Bruckheimer ：好的，明⽩了。谢谢。那么跟进问题 ——
Andrew Feldman ：Josh ，这已经是第七问了。
Joshua Bruckheimer：确实我的问题，我承认。能否谈谈甲⻣⽂和微软是如何向他们的客⼾定
位 Cerebras 的产品和服务？这是否主要体现为硬件销售，然后根据超快推理需求来驱动客⼾使

⽤量？谢谢。

## Document 337: Cliff Notes_ whiplash (2026-11-15)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Cliff Notes_ whiplash (2026-11-15).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 11
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: whiplash
By Ryan Wells & Elliot Clarke
11:20 November 06 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
In Australia, the RBA delivered another 25bp rate increase at its November meeting, taking the cash
rate to 4.85%. The move was widely expected, reflecting the Board’s continued concern around
inflation following the recent run higher in global energy prices, firmer domestic capacity pressures and

signs that inflation expectations have edged up. Inflation is now not expected to return to the mid-point
of the target band until mid-2028. This is despite weaker growth expectations and the unemployment

### Page 2

rate trending up to 5.1% by end-2028 on the RBA’s forecasts.
Importantly, though, the decision was not unanimous, with three members preferring to remain on hold
given downside risks to activity and the restraining influence of slower growth. Governor Bullock’s
suggestion that it was time to “take stock” after five rate increases also struck a less hawkish note,
while the statement dropped September’s explicit reference to raising rates further “if needed”. In our

view, February represents the next plausible opportunity for another hike, but we see this only as a risk.
Policy’s effect on housing is becoming increasingly visible. Cotality home prices fell a further 1.2% in
October to now be 6.7% below March’s peak. The cumulative price decline is approaching the scale of
previous housing corrections but appears to have regained momentum after September’s rate
increase. Dwelling approvals saw a 5.1% headline gain in September, but this was driven by volatile

unit approvals while detached house approvals fell sharply, suggesting higher rates and the
established-market downturn are beginning to weigh on the construction pipeline.
Official data on nominal household spending meanwhile rose 0.6% in September and 2.1% for Q3;
however, much of the recent acceleration looks to be price-driven, particularly for fuel. Real spending
volumes expanded by a much more modest (but still solid) 0.7% in Q3.
The Westpac-DataX Consumer Panel provides a granular look at income, spending and saving flows.

The latest update suggests that, beneath the resilient headline story, households are increasingly being
squeezed, particularly those with low savings buffers. With another rate increase now delivered,
housing wealth declining and sentiment weak, Q3 increasingly looks like the high-water mark for
growth in consumer spending.
Lastly for Australia, in September the goods trade surplus widened sharply to $2.8bn, with non-
monetary gold trade yet again adding considerable noise month-to-month. The most notable

development was another exceptionally strong result for data-processing equipment imports, taking the
Q3 total to $11.1bn. This is another clear marker of the scale of Australia’s data-centre investment
boom, which should feature prominently in both business investment and imports in the Q3 National
Accounts.
Offshore, the world has been focused on the US mid-term elections. In the House, Senate and a series
of State Governor races, the Democrats experienced resounding victory. Come 2027, the Democrats

will have a comfortable majority in both houses of Congress and are expected to make use of this
strength to respond to voter concerns, particularly over cost-of-living pressures. The election result also
limits President Trump’s ability to fund an extended military action against Iran. Notably, both
immediately before and after the election, President Trump and the administration have exhibited a
greater interest in furthering negotiations with Iran. It is therefore not a surprise that Brent oil has

backed off its most recent highs around USD118 last Friday, although the scale of the drop to near
USD93 yesterday is arguably too optimistic. Brent has since traded back up to above USD99, still 16%
below Friday’s peak.
While the initial reaction to the US election result was a robust broad-based rally in major equity
indexes, concerns over the potential for tighter regulation of AI and the related infrastructure buildout

### Page 3

Related articles
jolted US equities lower overnight – the Nasdaq falling 5.5% and S&P500 4.4%, both a multiple of the
prior session’s gains. That said, US term interest rates and FOMC rate expectations have also been
scaled back; so, while tech may come under greater scrutiny, a foundation for better outcomes across
the rest of the US economy may become evident in time. US data out this week continued to suggest
their economy is in robust health overall. And, if tensions in the Middle East abate, the FOMC’s

concerns over US inflation should subside. Gaining enough comfort for the FOMC to not hike in
December is a high bar, but the probability of further hikes in 2027 should continue to fall.
Turning then to the UK, the Bank of England raised Bank Rate by 25bps to 4.00% at their November
meeting as expected. On the Bank's baseline expectation, UK inflation is forecast to rise from 3.5%yr
at September to 5.00% by mid-2027. Activity data has also surprised to the upside in recent months,

though "there remains limited evidence of knock-on effects on other prices and wages".
In the press conference, Governor Bailey recognised the nascent improvement in energy price
pressures over the past 48 hours and the effectiveness of monetary policy but also conveyed that
"Unless progress towards resolving the conflict is made, or there is other news which softens the risk to
inflationary pressures, a further increase in Bank Rate may be warranted". Governor Bailey's tone and

comments made in the minutes by other Committee members did not suggest this was a decision that
needs to be made in December, but rather a risk to be judged as additional information is received.
This points to further tightening being more likely in early 2027 than before, and only if conditions / risks
warrant.

## Document 364: First Impressions_ NZ labour market surveys, September quarter 2026 (2026-11-15)

Source PDF: all report pdf/First Impressions_ NZ labour market surveys, September quarter 2026 (2026-11-15).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#labour #newzealand
First Impressions: NZ labour market surveys, September quarter
2026
By Michael Gordon
Senior Economist, Westpac NZ
09:01 November 04 2026 Share
The unemployment rate rose to 5.7% in the September quarter, against expectations for a
small decline. Spare capacity in the labour market is keeping wage pressures subdued.
Unemployment rate: 5.7% (prev: 5.6%, Westpac: 5.5%, RBNZ: 5.5%, mkt: 5.5%)
Employment change: -0.1% (prev: +0.3%, Westpac: +0.5%, RBNZ: +0.3%, mkt: +0.4%)

### Page 2

Participation rate: 70.4% (prev: 70.6%, Westpac: 70.8%, RBNZ: 70.6%, mkt: 70.7%) 
Labour costs (private sector): +0.5% (prev: +0.7%, Westpac: +0.5%, RBNZ: +0.4%, mkt: +0.5%)

The September quarter surveys pointed to ongoing softness in New Zealand’s labour market. The
unemployment rate rose from 5.6% to 5.7%, continuing an almost five-year run of increases.
Employment was softer than expected, and wage growth remains subdued despite increasing cost-of-
living pressures. 
The Household Labour Force Survey (HLFS) showed a surprise 0.1% fall in employment in the
September quarter, after a downwardly revised 0.3% increase in the June quarter. That went against
the Monthly Employment Indicator (MEI) measure of filled jobs, which had suggested some upward
momentum emerging in the jobs market (and notably, the HLFS measure had outpaced the MEI for the
previous nine quarters). It was also softer than suggested by the business-focused Quarterly
Employment Survey (QES), which recorded a 0.2% rise in full-time equivalent employees, a 0.1% rise
in filled jobs and a 0.6% rise in hours paid. 
The fall in HLFS employment was accompanied by a fall in labour force participation rate, from 70.6%
to 70.4%. These co-movements are effectively a reversal of the surprisingly large increases that we
saw in the June quarter, which we noted at the time were indicative of sampling error in the survey. 
This is why we generally recommend focusing on the unemployment rate, as it’s less vulnerable to
sampling error. And in that respect, the message on the jobs market remains weak: to the extent that
employment may be rising, it’s still not fast enough to absorb the growth in the population (which is
itself a fairly low bar, as net migration remains at a relatively low ebb). 
The HLFS also recorded a sharp 1.6% fall in hours worked (although usual hours worked were down
just 0.4%). This seems to have been influenced by Matariki – a floating holiday that was only
introduced a few years ago, so will not be picked up by the seasonal adjustment. This may have also
been responsible for a marked shift from full-time to part-time employment, as some people may have
been pushed into the part-time category simply by virtue of only working four days during the survey
week. 
The Labour Cost Index (LCI) rose by 0.5% in the September quarter, in line with our expectations
overall but a bit weaker in the details – private sector wages were up by 0.5%, while there was a
surprisingly large 0.7% increase in public sector wages. There was a notable jump in the number of
employers citing cost-of-living adjustments as a reason for increasing pay rates, although the size of
those increases varied a great deal, with many substantially less than the headline inflation rate. 
Overall, we think the details are soft from the RBNZ’s perspective – an important consideration ahead
of the 9 December Monetary Policy Statement, as there are few other major data releases between
now and then. The unemployment rate is a more reliable signal than the employment measures, and
the ongoing rise in unemployment points to more slack in the labour market than the RBNZ was hoping
to see in an economy that has been growing for over a year now. The doves on the policy committee
will be encouraged in their view that there is limited scope for headline inflation shocks to pass through
into second-round effects. 
Michael Gordon, Senior Economist
P: +64 21 749 506

## Document 413: Polish economic data supports our call for rate hikes in early 2027

Source PDF: all report pdf/Polish economic data supports our call for rate hikes in early 2027.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-13
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Poland’s economy slowed in the third quarter, while core
inﬂation fell below 3% YoY. There is still little evidence of
second-round eﬀects. The MPC has nevertheless turned more
hawkish with staﬀ projecting high inﬂation next year,
supporting our base case of 50bp of rate hikes in early 2027

Rafal Benecki and Adam Antoniak
Poland’s economy slowed in the third quarter
GDP growth slowed in 3Q26, similar to
other economies in the region
According to the preliminary estimate, GDP growth slowed to 3.5%
YoY in 3Q26 from 4.1% YoY in 2Q26. Seasonally adjusted data point
to a slowdown in growth to 0.8% QoQ from 1.1% QoQ in the
previous quarter. We will learn the breakdown of GDP growth for
3Q26 on 30 November, but the available data suggests that growth
in industrial gross value added slowed, while construction activity
declined in annual terms. Continued solid growth in services was
not enough to oﬀset weaker performance in these areas.

### Page 2

On the expenditure side, the main reason for slower growth last
quarter compared with 2Q26 was a deterioration in net exports, ie,
the foreign trade balance. Net exports added 0.5ppt to annual GDP
growth in 2Q26, while we estimate that the contribution turned
slightly negative in 3Q26. Private consumption growth probably
slowed further as well. With nominal wage growth normalising and
inﬂation rising, household incomes came under pressure. Higher

spending on fuel is limiting households’ ability to increase
consumption elsewhere. Geopolitical uncertainty also remains
elevated, resulting in a persistently high propensity to save.
Slower GDP growth, including weaker consumer demand, is an
important factor limiting inﬂationary pressure. This marks a
signiﬁcant diﬀerence from the previous energy shock in 2022, which
was accompanied by strong income growth and a consumption
boom. Under current conditions, it is more diﬃcult for companies to

raise prices and pass higher energy costs on to consumers. Price
competition in retail also remains intense, while Poland continues
to face strong competitive pressure from Chinese imports.

### Page 3

According to the ﬁnal estimate, CPI inﬂation fell to 3.5% YoY in
October from 4.1% YoY in September. This was mainly due to a
decline in fuel prices (-9.7% MoM), related to cuts to excise duty and
VAT on petrol and diesel. Growth in food and non-alcoholic
beverage prices also remained low at 0.4% MoM, although we are
approaching the end of the period of annual deﬂation in this
category that began in June this year. The impact of the energy
crisis was, however, visible in housing energy prices, which rose by

1.7% MoM, mainly because of higher prices for solid fuels (7.4%
MoM, including an 11.2% MoM rise in coal) and liquid fuels (2.9%
MoM). Goods prices rose by 2.9% YoY and services prices by 4.9%
YoY, compared with 3.6% YoY and 5.3% YoY, respectively, in
September.
The biggest surprise in the October inﬂation breakdown, however,
was the decline in core inﬂation excluding food and energy prices.
According to our current estimates, it fell to 2.9% YoY from 3.1% YoY

in September, dropping below 3% YoY for the ﬁrst time since March.
Annual inﬂation in the recreation, sport and culture category fell
markedly, from 5.5% YoY to 4.0% YoY, mainly due to more
favourable tourism prices. Price pressure from transport services
also eased. Air fares fell by 10% MoM, although they remain 10%
higher than a year ago.
The higher energy commodity prices are not translating into
broader price pressures in the Polish economy, while the risk of

second-round eﬀects, ie, wage increases in response to inﬂation,
remains low.

Download
The National Bank of Poland’s (NBP) November inﬂation projection
points to a high inﬂation path in 2027, prompting a signiﬁcant

hawkish shift in the Monetary Policy Council (MPC) rhetoric. Recent
comments from Council members indicate a willingness to raise
interest rates in early 2027. This is consistent with our baseline
monetary policy scenario, which assumes a 50bp increase in the
cost of money in 1Q27.
The NBP remains more dovish than other central banks in the
region and core market policymakers, including the Fed and ECB,
and in our view could postpone rate hikes until early 2027. Data on

the October CPI composition conﬁrm that there is still no evidence
of second-round eﬀects. It's worth noting that monetary policy was
also more restrictive at the starting point than in other countries.
We believe the Council is waiting for information on the scale of
government measures to cushion electricity, gas and fuel prices at
the beginning of 2027.
The inﬂation scenario presented by NBP economic staﬀ is, however,
subject to considerable uncertainty. This applies to fuel prices on

global and Polish markets, as well as the scale of changes in
regulated energy prices, including electricity and gas bills, from the
beginning of 2027. Oil prices have moderated again, while the
government is preparing measures to freeze gas prices for
households.

The scenario, forecasts and nowcasts of the Economic Research

### Page 2

UNITED STATES 
The US economy is expected to grow above its potential in 2026, with an average annual growth rate of 2.2%, close to the 2025 rate (2.1%). 
This apparent resilience to the uncertainty, tariff and energy shocks, together with above-trend productivity growth, masks K-shaped growth, 
driven by investment linked to AI-optimism and consumption by the wealthiest amid a wealth effect (historically high stock market valuations). 
Inflation overshooting is set to continue (3.5% in 2026) at least through 2028, largely because of the rise in oil prices and tariffs – although the 
impact of these latter appears to be less significant than expected. The labour market is showing clear signs of improvement that should bring 
the unemployment rate down toward 4.0%. Given this shift in price and employment risks amid dynamic growth, the FOMC proceeded with a 
first rate hike at September’s meeting and we expect two further 25bp increases in December 2026 and January 2027, bringing the Fed Funds 
target range at 4.25% - 4.50%.

CHINA 
Economic growth rebounded to 4.5% y/y in Q3 2026 (1.2% q/q) after 4.3% y/y in Q2 and 5.0% in Q1. It is projected to reach 4.6% in 2026 as a 
whole, down from 5% in 2025. Growth remains characterised by a K-shaped trajectory, and the gap between the strong performance of exports 
and the fragility of sectors that rely on domestic demand has widened this year. Retail sales barely increased in the first nine months of 2026, 
the crisis in the property sector has continued and household confidence remains low. Fiscal policy measures aimed at supporting investment 
and private consumption will increase over the last months of the year. CPI inflation has rebounded slightly since last March, mostly due to 
the rise in global energy prices, as well as due to the increase in electronic good prices and anti-involution measures implemented by the 
authorities. Deflationary pressures persist, however, given weakness in domestic demand.

EUROZONE 
The economy has shown resilience in the face of the energy shock: GDP grew by 0.4% q/q in Q3, and the 1.0% growth carry-over for 2026 
supports our forecast of 1.1% for the year as a whole (compared to 1.3% in 2025) with the risk tilted to the upside. Growth is expected to 
strengthen to 1.6% in 2027, bolstered by increased spending on defence and infrastructure, as well as the global investment cycle in AI. Annual 
average inflation is thus expected to reach 3.1% in 2026 (from 2.1% in 2025), and 3.2% in 2027. Given persistent inflation and resilient growth, 
we anticipate another 25bp hike from the ECB in December 2026, bringing the deposit rate to 2.75%, followed by an extended pause. That 
said, stronger than expected second-round effects could lead to more tightening than our current baseline.

stephane.colliac@bnpparibas.com

### Page 3

FRANCE 
GDP contracted by 0.1% q/q in Q3 2026 (following stagnation in Q2), weighed down by a decline in aeronautics production (delay in the 
delivery of inputs) but supported by service sector output and purchases of electric vehicles. Overall, growth is expected to slow to 0.3% in 
2026 (after 0.9% in 2025) before rebounding to 0.8% in 2027. In parallel, inflation should reach 2.5% in 2026 (compared with 0.9% in 2025), 
before edging down slightly in 2027 (2.4%).

UNITED KINGDOM 
UK GDP growth would improve slightly to 1.3% in 2026 and remain at this level in 2027 (1.2% in 2025). The economy has so far weathered 
both the energy price shock and domestic political volatility. That said, growth is expected to decelerate in the second half of the year, while 
inflation is set to remain stubbornly above target (averaging 3.2% in 2026 and 3.5% in 2027), driven by higher energy prices and mounting 
second-round effects. In response, the BoE has finally raised its Bank Rate to 4.00%; If a second-rate hike looks likely for February, our baseline 
scenario projects that easing will resume in the second half of 2027, with two cuts taking the Bank Rate down to 3.50% by year‑end.

JAPAN 
Following 1.2% in 2025, Japan's GDP growth is expected to decelerate to 1.0% in 2026, before rebounding to 1.2% in 2027. Higher inflation 
and production costs associated with the energy shock are expected to weigh on the economy’s overall performance. On the other hand, the 
latter is supported by fiscal policy and AI-related investment. Inflation has generally overshot the 2% y/y target since 2022. Accordingly, the 
Bank of Japan initiated a cautious process of “adjustment in the degree of monetary accommodation” in 2024, lifting the policy rate to 1.25% 
so far (previously negative) – the highest since 1995. We expect two more hikes by the end of Q1 2027, followed by a return to a more cautious 
pace until the terminal rate of +2.5% is reached in H2 2028. Japan is facing long-term rates pressure, illustrated by historically high 10- and 30-
year yields, probably fueled by the level of public debt and the pace of monetary adjustment.

EXCHANGE RATES 
We expect Fed hikes to temporarily offset structural headwinds and support the dollar. EUR/USD would target 1.14 by end-2026, before the 
dollar's structural downtrend resumes toward 1.20 by end-2027, although political and fiscal risk may weigh on the euro next year. The USD/JPY 
appreciation would continue but at a more moderate pace, with the BoJ still behind the curve despite its hikes. By contrast, GBP/USD would 
see relative support underpinned by a modest fiscal risk premium under the new Labour government, and resilient growth.

The Week Ahead October 23, 2026

CENTRAL BANK MEETINGS

Bank of Thailand (BOT) (October 28)

We expect the BOT to maintain its policy 
rate at 1.00% at its 28 October meeting, 
extending its pause for a fourth 
consecutive decision as it balances 
inflationary pressures against uneven 
and subdued economic growth. Our view 
is aligned with fixed market income 
pricing. Headline inflation rose anew to 
2.8% yoy in September 2026, driven by 
higher energy, food, and core price 
increases, but remained within the upper 
end of the central bank’s 1-3% target 
range. As the BOT is still likely to look 
through what it views as temporary, 
supply-driven inflationary pressures, it 
will likely refrain from tightening policy, 
and keep interest rates accommodative 
to support the soft and uneven growth 
cycle.

European Central Bank (October 29)

We expect the ECB to extend its pause, 
keeping the deposit facility rate at 2.5%. 
Market participants will instead focus on 
the central bank’s assessment of rising 
French and Italian bond yields and 
widening spreads versus Bunds. While 
we do not expect direct references to 
individual countries, the ECB is likely to 
signal that recent developments in 
France reflect the country’s weaker 
fiscal and debt profile rather than a 
broader systemic concern. Policymakers 
will seek to differentiate between a 
localised bond sell-off and a Eurozone-
wide market disruption, justifying their 
decision to monitor developments rather 
than implement specific measures at this 
juncture. Meanwhile, inflation continues 
to rise, with little relief expected in 
upcoming readings given elevated 
global fuel prices. 
Bank of Japan (October 30)

The Bank of Japan is expected to keep 
the overnight call rate unchanged at 1.25% 
at this meeting, pausing after the 25bp 
hike in September. The BOJ is likely to 
assess the impact of the previous rate 
hike before taking further action. The 
recent stabilisation of the yen also 
reduces the urgency of another hike at 
this meeting.

The tightening bias should remain intact. 
Growth and inflation remain broadly on 
track, leaving room for the BOJ to revise 
up its FY2026–FY2027 GDP and CPI 
forecasts. Wage growth has also 
remained strong, with Rengo reportedly 
targeting wage increases of 6% in 2027, 
reinforcing the BOJ’s confidence that 
underlying inflation can be sustained 
around its 2% target. We continue to 
expect the policy rate to rise to 1.50% in 
December 2026 or January 2027, 
followed by a further increase to 1.75% 
by 2Q 2027.

FORTHCOMING DATA 
RELEASES

Hong Kong SAR

Export growth is expected to accelerate 
from 53.0% yoy in August to 55.0% in 
September, in line with the pickup in 
China’s export growth from 25.0% to 
36.3%. As a key re-export hub for China,

### Page 3

The Week Ahead October 23, 2026 
                                                                                                                                         3

Hong Kong should benefit from robust 
AI-related electronics trade, with 
electrical machinery, equipment and 
parts exports to Hong Kong accounting 
for 20.6% of China’s total exports. 
Import growth is also expected to remain 
firm, supported by resilient export 
orders and gradually improving 
investment sentiment, sustaining 
demand for intermediate and capital 
goods.

Singapore

Singapore’s industrial production (IP) 
likely remained in expansion for a 13th 
consecutive month in September 2026, 
although we expect growth to moderate 
to 7.6% yoy from August’s strong 15.4% 
yoy pace. IP growth continued to be 
underpinned by the electronics and 
precision engineering clusters, which 
has been key drivers of Singapore’s 
manufacturing strength, reflecting 
sustained demand for artificial 
intelligence-related hardware. However, 
performance across clusters remained 
uneven, with chemicals and biomedical 
manufacturing likely contracting, and 
tempering overall gains.

South Korea

The preliminary estimate for 3Q26 GDP 
is likely to show growth remaining solid 
at 3.2% yoy, slightly below 3.7% in 2Q 
and 3.8% in 1Q. Nominal GDP growth is 
likely to remain in double digits, 
significantly outpacing real GDP growth, 
thanks to improved terms of trade amid 
rising memory export prices. By 
expenditure, exports picked up in 3Q in 
both nominal and real terms, supported 
by stronger semiconductor shipments. 
Investment also appeared robust, as 
reflected in the equipment investment 
estimation index and machinery order 
index. Private consumption, however, 
appeared to weaken amid the sharp 
KOSPI correction since July following 
the introduction of leveraged ETF 
regulations. Our full-year GDP growth 
forecasts of 3.5% for 2026 and 2.4% for 
2027 remain on track.

Taiwan

The preliminary estimate for 3Q GDP is 
likely to show growth remaining strong 
at 12–13% yoy, close to 12.9% in 2Q, 
albeit below the peak of 15.4% in 1Q. 
Exports maintained strong double-digit 
growth in 3Q (44.6% yoy in nominal 
terms and 21.1% in real terms), supported 
by robust shipments of semiconductors, 
servers and other AI-related products. 
Machinery investment also sustained 
strong double-digit growth, underpinned 
by higher capacity utilisation in the ICT 
sector and a resulting increase in capital 
goods imports. Private consumption 
appeared to have strengthened further, 
supported by stock market gains, 
improving wage growth, and the 
associated wealth and income effects. 
Construction investment remained the 
weak spot, weighed down by elevated 
inventories in the new-home market and 
the ongoing need for inventory digestion. 
We maintain our 2026 and 2027 GDP 
growth forecasts at 11.6% and 5.6%, 
respectively, with risks tilted to the 
upside.

## Document 501: 【量化专题】天风库存因子系列（一）：库存水平与渠道口径

Source PDF: all report pdf/【量化专题】天风库存因子系列（一）：库存水平与渠道口径.pdf
Report source: 紫金天风
Date: 2026-11-13
Pages: 31
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

研究报告 | 专题报告  紫金天风期货研究所 
 1 / 31 
紫金天风期货股份有限公司依法已获取期货交易咨询业务资格。 
【量化专题】天风库存因子系
列（一）：库存水平与渠道口径 
2026 年 11 月 10 日

观点小结 
数据来自天风风云数据库中的库存相关序列。本系列从中选取库存水平、社会库存、
港口库存、厂库与库存天数等基础指标，每个品种保留一条代表序列，再按绩效与低相关
原则筛选入选因子。最终得到 22 个因子。本篇聚焦其中库存水平与渠道口径的 6 个：核
心库存、社会库存、港口库存、厂内库存、库存周转天数与原料覆盖天数。 
各因子按统一规则生成每日多空仓位；多数做时序择时，厂内库存做截面多空。基本
面按天风服务器更新时间对齐到交易日并滞后一日；成交按当日收盘价，双边成本万分之
一；评价自 2016 年 1 月起；先验为库存偏高则做空。 
在滚动训练窗口内选参并固定执行；多因子在品种层合成后，有仓品种等权配置。本
篇合成组合夏普约 1.54，年化约 7.29%，最大回撤约 -5.97%。

1 样本与方法 
样本覆盖国内商品期货主要品种。库存序列来自天风风云数据库相关数据，按服务器
更新时间对齐交易日，标准化或变换后滞后一日交易；按当日收盘成交，双边成本万分之
一，评价自 2016 年 1 月起。

作者：聂波 
从业资格证号：F03117695       
交易咨询证号：Z0019358 
联系方式：niebo@zjtfqh.com 
研究助理：王隆 
从业资格证号：F03159955 
联系方式：wanglong@zjtfqh.com 
审核人：李文涛 
交易咨询证号：Z0015640

### Page 2

研究报告 | 专题报告  紫金天风期货研究所
2 / 31
紫金天风期货股份有限公司依法已获取期货交易咨询业务资格。
各因子按统一规则生成每日多空仓位。时序策略根据库存高低做多空择时；截面策略
按当日相对强弱分处两侧。有仓品种等权配置。
单因子在有仓品种上归一得到日度权重；多因子在品种层合成信号，详见后文。在滚
动训练窗口内选参。
2 本篇因子总览
2.1 数据频率与交易日对齐
库存多为周度或更低频观测，策略按交易日定仓与记账。在滚动训练窗口内选参后执
行；总体绩效、合仓与分年结果均按此口径。
慢频库存以天风服务器更新时间为可得时点：仅当更新时间不晚于当日决策截止，该
观测才进入信息集；交易日网格取截止前最新值，标准化或变换后再滞后一个交易日，按
当日收盘成交；公布间隔内仓位沿用既有信号。另以市场总持仓环境做分位稳健性检验。
下表为本篇主口径绩效，评价自 2016 年起。单因子为各自训练窗口选参后的策略路
径；合仓行对各因子信号在品种层合成后，有仓品种等权配置。卡玛为年化收益除以最大
回撤绝对值。

表 1：库存水平与渠道口径本篇因子与合仓绩效（主口径，2016 年起）
因子 夏普 年化 最大回撤 卡玛

社会库存 1.105 11.89% -11.13% 1.068
港口库存 0.714 4.50% -15.26% 0.295
厂内库存 0.732 6.24% -19.31% 0.323
库存周转天数 0.847 10.34% -23.82% 0.434

仓夏普约 1.544，同设定单因子最高约 1.381。

3 分因子构建、逻辑与实证 
3.1 核心库存 
构建：取天风风云数据库中的核心库存相关序列，各品种选用覆盖面尽可能完整的可
见库存口径。按服务器更新时间对齐到交易日后，对库存水平做滚动标准化，并滞后一日
进入时序择时；库存偏高偏空、偏低偏多，有仓品种等权合成为多品种组合。 
下表给出 2016 年起出现过有效非零信号的品种。 
表 2：核心库存品种池（2016 年起有效信号） 
品种数 品种池 
38 LPG、塑料、聚丙烯、PTA、PVC、不锈钢、乙二醇、豆一、尿素、郑棉、棕榈
油、橡胶、沥青、烧碱、热轧卷板、焦炭、焦煤、燃油、玉米、玉米淀粉、玻璃、
甲醇、短纤、纯碱、纸浆、苯乙烯、苹果、菜油、菜粕、螺纹钢、豆油、豆粕、铁
矿石、沪铅、沪铜、沪铝、沪锌、沪镍 
数据来源：紫金天风期货研究所 
交易方式为日度时序择时；变换方式为滚动标准化；先验方向为反向（指标偏高则做
空）。 
经济逻辑：库存偏高意味着近端供给相对宽松，价格承压。覆盖面最广，作为库存主
题的主锚。

经济逻辑：社库升高往往对应渠道累库、现货偏松，与核心库存同向承压，但更贴近
渠道博弈与近月定价。

构建：港口库存覆盖进口品与沿海集散港口库存；各品种优先采用全国或区域汇总口
径，无汇总时再对分港序列加总。滚动标准化并滞后一日后做时序择时，有仓品种等权。

3.4 厂内库存 
构建：同品种多口径时优先更宽覆盖，否则取均值；对齐交易日后滚动标准化并滞后
一日，按截面相对强弱分处多空，有仓品种等权。 
下表给出 2016 年起出现过有效非零信号的品种。 
表 11：厂内库存品种池（2016 年起有效信号） 
品种数 品种池 
19 螺纹钢、焦煤、聚丙烯、尿素、短纤、LPG、烧碱、碳酸锂、纯碱、PTA、铁矿

数据来源：紫金天风期货研究所 
交易方式为日度截面多空；变换方式为滚动标准化（更长窗）；先验方向为反向（指
标偏高则做空）。 
经济逻辑：厂内库存偏高，通常对应生产端被动累库或需求接货偏弱，近端宽松、价
格承压。覆盖黑色钢厂、化工与农产品样本企业厂库。

4 品种层信号合成组合 
4.1 如何合成 
每个因子先形成日期×品种的多空信号。本篇合成信号默认采用训练窗口选参。同一品
种上，当日有信号的因子取平均得到合成得分，再按得分绝对值之和归一为品种权重，方
向由得分符号决定。 
本篇共 6 个因子参与合成。合成后夏普 1.544，年化 7.29%，最大回撤 -5.97%。 
4.2 策略相关性 
下图给出各单因子策略日收益的两两相关，用于观察不同渠道口径的盈亏是否同步、
合仓是否仍有分散空间。 
图 19：库存水平与渠道口径因子策略日收益相关矩阵

5.2 多因子等权组合 
在单因子路径之上，将本篇各渠道口径于品种层等权合成，再按得分绝对值之和归一
得到品种权重与方向。合仓目标是多渠道信息综合与风险分散，不宜与某一单因子在特定
设定下的峰值夏普直接对标；单因子高点可作为参照，不构成合仓的上界。下图按训练窗
口分组，图例区分持有期。

今月の景気判断 ～持ち直している
【今月のポイント】
 東海経済は、持ち直している
 7月の生産（東海3県、季節調整値）は前月
比＋2.2％と2ヵ月ぶりに増加した。生産水
準も高く、増加基調にあるとみられる
 輸出は総じてみると足踏み状態。先行きは
持ち直しを見込むが、米・イランの対立長
期化などで足踏みとなるリスクがある
 日銀短観9月調査の業況判断DI（「良い」
－「悪い」）は、製造業が改善する一方、非
製造業は悪化した。設備投資も前年度比
増加が見込まれている
 8月の小売販売額は名目で2ヵ月連続で
前年比プラスとなったが、実質では3ヵ月
連続でマイナス。一方、新車販売台数は
5ヵ月連続で前年比プラスとなった
現状

生産
7月の鉱工業生産（東海3県）は前月比＋2.2％と2ヵ月ぶりに増加した。6月に電子部品・デバイス、輸送機械
が減少し全体を引き下げたが、7月は電子部品・デバイスが増加に転じた。東海の生産は、全体として水準も
高く、増加基調にあるとみられる。先行きについては高水準での推移を見込むが、米・イランの対立長期化に
より下振れるリスクもある。

企業景況感（日銀短観）
日銀短観9月調査の業況判断DIは、全産業で＋22ptと6月調査(＋19pt)から改善した。製造業では、非鉄金
属、生産用機械、電気機械、自動車等が改善し、全体でも改善となる一方、非製造業では建設、小売、情報
通信などが悪化し、全体としては小幅悪化したものの、引き続き高い水準を維持している。先行き（12月）につ
いては、製造業、非製造業とも悪化が見込まれている。

個人消費（小売売上、自動車販売）
8月の小売販売額（名目）は前年比＋0.2％と小幅ながら2ヵ月連続でプラスとなった。実質値を試算する

生産
8月の鉱工業生産（関西）は前月比－2.6%と2ヵ月連続でマイナスとなり、足踏み状態となっている。主要業種
の動きをみると、化学が増加したが、電子部品・デバイス、電気機械、汎用・生産用・業務用機械が減少した。
先行きは、持ち直しを見込むが、米・イランの対立長期化、中国経済の減速などから、下振れるリスクがある。

日銀短観9月調査の業況判断DI（「良い」－「悪い」）は、全産業で＋21ptと6月調査（＋17ｐｔ）から改善。製
造業、非製造業ともに改善した。業種別に見ると、製造業では化学、非鉄金属、はん用・生産用・業務用機

## Document 522: スペインは解散総選挙へ

Source PDF: all report pdf/スペインは解散総選挙へ.pdf
Report source: MURC
Date: 2026-10-07
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

2026 年 10 月 7 日

MURC Focus

スペインは解散総選挙へ

～右派連立政権が成立する見込み～

調査部 主任研究員 土田 陽介

1. 解散総選挙に臨んだサンチェス首相 
スペインのペドロ・サンチェス首相が 10 月 5 日、議会を解散し、11 月 29 日に総選挙（定数 350、比例代表制）
を行うと発表した。住宅価格や家賃の高騰に対する政府の緊急政令案が、10 月 2 日に下院で否決されたことを
受けて、少数与党による政権運営の行き詰まりを意識した首相が、国民に信を問う決断をした形である。ただし、
サンチェス首相が率いる中道左派の与党・社会労働党（PSOE）の支持率は低迷している（図表 1）。

もともと PSOE の人気は低迷しており、前回 2023 年 7 月の総選挙でも、ライバル政党である中道右派の国民
党（PP）が第一党となっていた。しかしサンチェス首相は他の左派政党や地域政党の協力を受けて組閣し、政権
を維持した経緯がある。しかし首相周辺の汚職疑惑などから PSOE の人気は回復せず、次期の総選挙では PP
と同根の右派政党である Vox による連立政権が成立する公算が大きい。 
図表 1. 主要政党の支持率調査 図表 2. スペインの住宅価格

（出所）各種世論調査より

（出所）欧州連合統計局（ユーロスタット）

ユーロ圏 スペイン
（2015年=100、季調済）
（年、四半期）
〇スペインのサンチェス首相が10月5日、議会を解散し、11月29日に総選挙を行うと発表した。次期の総選挙
では野党第一党の PP と同根の右派政党 Vox による連立政権が成立する公算が大きい。 
〇PP と Vox が総選挙で勝利すれば、2018年6月以来の政権交代が実現する。こうした右派回帰を民族主義
の台頭という流れに位置付ける向きもあるが、スペインの国内事情はもっと複雑である。 
〇仮に総選挙を経て PP と Vox の連立政権が成立しても、両派間ですり合わせるべき課題も多く、スペイン政
治が安定するとは限らない。ゆえに、当面の問題への対応も進まないだろう。

### Page 2

解散の直接的な理由となった住宅価格の問題であるが、住宅市況が底にあった 2015 年を基準（＝100）とす
る指数でその動きを確認すると、直近 2026 年 4～6 月期は 201 と、ユーロ圏全体（159）を大きく上回る（図表 2）。
特に 2023 年以降の上昇が目覚ましいが、堅調な景気や移民の急増を受けて住宅需要が高まったことがその主
因である。

2. 民族主義的な右派回帰とは異なる 
PP と Vox が総選挙で勝利すれば、2018 年 6 月以来の政権交代が実現する。こうした右派回帰を民族主義
の台頭という流れに位置付ける向きもあるが、スペインの国内事情は複雑であるため、必ずしもその流れに合わ
ない点、留意すべきである。まず、そもそも PSOE の人気そのものが低下している。確かに景気は堅調だが、住
宅価格の急騰のような社会問題に関して、サンチェス政権は具体的な処方箋を提示できなかった。

総選挙を経て政権交代が実現し、PP が Vox と連立を組んだとしても、それを民族主義の台頭の表れと理解す
るよりも、一定の期間ごとに繰り返される左派から右派へのリバランスの流れとして理解するほうが適切だろう。ま
たスペインの場合、右派間に対立関係があることにも注目する必要がある。地域ナショナリズムが強いスペインで
は地方分権を求める保守の力が強く、中央集権を主張する保守（つまり Vox）と対立関係にある。

中央集権を望む保守の一部に戦前・戦後のフランコ体制を理想化する向きがあることも、地域ナショナリズムと
の対立を生んでいる。地域ナショナリズム政党の中には、自らを保守と位置付けながらも、中央集権を望む保守と
の対立から、PSOE や他の左派政党（Podemos や Sumar）と歩調を合わせる動きもある。スペインの民族主義は
多様なため、同国の右派回帰を反欧州連合（EU）や反移民の文脈で捉えることはできない。

例えばスペイン南東部のカタルーニャ州では、伝統的に左派も右派も民族主義を掲げ、独立運動（あるいは高
度な自治の獲得のための運動）を展開しているが、同時に票を奪い合う関係にある。最近は左派のカタルーニャ
共和主義左翼（ERC）が劣勢である。一方、右派のカタルーニャ同盟（AC）が優勢となっており、政党支持率では
PSOE に次ぐ第 2 位となっている。

AC は右派だが独立を主張しており、中央集権を主張する Vox とは対立する。一方、カタルーニャ州でも独立
に同調しない有権者は数多く、そうした有権者は PSOE に投票する傾向が強い。カタルーニャ州はスペインでも
有数の産業地帯であるため、労働運動が盛んだったことから、伝統的に PSOE の支持が厚いためである。このよ
うに、スペイン政治はまだら模様であるため、右派回帰と民族主義を同一視することは不適切である。

### Page 3

3. 総選挙後の新政権も多難な船出となる見込み 
総選挙後にいかなる立場の新政権が成立しようとも、スペイン経済の先行きに対する懸念は残る。確かに景気
は堅調を維持しているが、一方、冒頭で述べた住宅価格の高騰のような社会問題に対する妙薬はなかなか存在
しない。加えて、経済成長の次なる牽引役を見いだせていないという問題もある。例えばスペイン景気を牽引する
輸出だが、旅行サービスに支えられたサービスの輸出は好調でもモノの輸出は不調である（図表 3）。

中南米からの移民による労働供給が潤沢とはいえ、グローバルな労働力不足やスペインの人口構成（純増で
はあるが移民流入に依存した増加）に鑑みれば、スペインでも生産性の向上は不可欠である。それに資する総固
定資本形成は、実体として建設投資に偏っており、機械投資や知的財産投資の規模は限定的である（図表 4）。
これらの投資を増やすためには国による産業政策が欠かせない。

具体的には、規制緩和や税制優遇といった措置である。これまではサンチェス首相率いる PSOE 政権が少数
与党だったということや、PSOE が中道左派であるため経済成長よりも所得分配を重視したこともあり、そうした設
備投資を後押しするような政策が必ずしも打ち出されたわけではなかった。では PP と Vox の連立政権に交代し
たことでそれが可能になるかどうかというと、その可能性も高くない。

なぜならば、PP と Vox の連立政権が強いイニシアチブの下で産業政策を“上から”推し進めようとすると、中央
集権的な色彩が強くなり、地域ナショナリズムとの間であつれきが生じる展開が予想されるためである。中央集権
志向を弱めるという現実的な判断を Vox ができるかどうかが鍵を握るが、同党にはもともと PP から右派色の濃い
グループが離脱して結党した経緯があるだけに、その余地がどの程度か定かではない。

現状では総選挙を経て PSOE から PP と Vox の連立へと政権が交代する公算が大きいが、両党ですり合わせ
るべき課題も多く、政治が安定するとは限らない。ゆえに、当面の問題への対応も進まないだろう。 
図表 3. GDP に占める輸出の割合 図表 4. GDP に占める固定資本形成の割合

## Document 537: ロシア中銀、２会合連続の金利据え置き、インフレ再燃で利下げは遠のくか ～燃料供給制約と期待インフレの高止まりが重しに、低_ba60bdfd

Source PDF: all report pdf/ロシア中銀、２会合連続の金利据え置き、インフレ再燃で利下げは遠のくか ～燃料供給制約と期待インフレの高止まりが重しに、低_ba60bdfd.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-26
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

World Trends

2026 年10 月 26 日

1/4 
ロシア中銀、２会合連続の金利据え置き、インフレ再燃で利下げは遠のくか 
～燃料供給制約と期待インフレの高止まりが重しに、低成長下でも高金利が長期化する公算～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  西濵 徹 
（℡：050-5474-7495）

（要旨） 
○ ロシア中銀は 10 月 23 日の会合で、政策金利を２会合連続で 14.00％に据え置いた。2025 年
６月から 10 会合連続で利下げを進めてきたものの、インフレ再加速の兆しを受けて警戒姿
勢を維持した。９月のインフレ率は前年比＋6.35％、コアインフレ率も同＋5.47％で、とも
に目標の４％を上回る。中銀は 2026 年末のインフレ見通しを＋7.1～7.6％に上方修正し、
2027 年の平均政策金利見通しも12.5～13.5％へ引き上げるなど利下げ余地は縮小している。 
○ インフレ再燃の主因は国内の供給制約である。ウクライナによる製油所へのドローン攻撃で
石油精製量が減り、全土でガソリン不足と価格上昇が起きているほか、10 月の公共料金引き
上げも物価を押し上げている。原油高による輸出収入がルーブルを支える一方、国内の燃料
高は解消せず、期待インフレや企業の価格見通しは高止まりしている。中銀は、供給ショッ
クが価格設定や賃金交渉に織り込まれる二次的波及を防ぐ狙いがあるとみられる。 
○ 景気面では、2026 年の成長率見通しは＋0.5～1.0％と小幅に上方修正されるも低水準にとど
まり、2027 年は＋1.0～2.0％へ下方修正された。人手不足など供給能力の制約から、低成長
下でも物価上昇圧力が残りやすく、景気の弱さを理由にした追加利下げのハードルは高まっ
ている。財政面でも、2027 年度予算案を中銀は慎重と評価する一方、歳出拡大を可能にする
法律が成立しており、追加歳出が利下げ余地をさらに狭める可能性がある。 
○ ナビウリナ総裁は引き締め姿勢の長期維持が必要との見方を示し、2027 年には利下げ余地が
生じ得るとしつつも、インフレが想定より高止まりする可能性にも言及した。次回会合は 12
月 18 日に予定されている。供給制約の緩和と期待インフレの低下が進めば、利下げ再開の余
地は広がるものの、供給障害が長引けば据え置きが長期化するとみられる。

【ロシア中銀は２会合連続で利下げ休止を継続、インフレへの警戒姿勢を維持】 
ロシア中央銀行は、10 月 23 日に開催した定例会合において、政策金利を２会合連続で 14.00％
に据え置くことを決定した。中銀は 2025 年６月から 2026 年７月まで 10 会合連続で利下げを実施
し、政策金利を 21.00％から 14.00％まで引き下げてきた。しかし、９月の前回会合では11 会合ぶ

### Page 2

2/4 
りに利下げを休止しており（注１）、今回も据え置きを継続した。 
背景には、2025 年初旬を境に鈍化して
きたインフレに再加速の兆しが出てい
ることがある（図１）。９月のインフレ率
は前年同月比＋6.35％となり、前月の同
＋6.33％からほぼ横ばいとなったもの
の、中銀目標（４％）を上回る伸びで推
移しており、過去数ヵ月は緩やかに加速
の動きを強めている。コアインフレ率は
前年同月比＋5.47 ％ と 前 月 （ 同 ＋
5.37％）から伸びが加速しており、５月
を境に加速に転じている。 
こうした状況を踏まえて、中銀は 2026 年末のインフレ率見通しを＋7.1～7.6％と従来見通し（＋
6.0～7.0％）から上方修正している。なお、2027 年末にはインフレ率が目標の４％に回帰する見通
しを維持しているものの、その実現に必要となる 2027 年の政策金利の平均水準を 12.5～13.5％と
従来見通し（10.5～12.5％）から引き上げており、先行きの利下げ余地が縮小していると捉えられ
る。その意味では、物価上昇圧力が残るなかで金融引き締め的な状態を長く維持する姿勢を示した。

【供給制約による燃料価格の上昇と期待インフレの高止まりが金融緩和の制約要因に】 
足元でインフレが再燃している背景には、国内における供給制約が高まっていることがある。ウ
クライナによる製油所をはじめとするエネルギー・輸送インフラへのドローン攻撃の強化を受け、
ロシア国内では石油精製量が減少し、ガソリンや軽油などの供給が制約されている。政府は、備蓄
分のガソリン放出のほか、規制撤廃による低品質燃料の販売許可、軽油の輸出禁止などにより国内
供給を優先させている。にもかかわらず、石油製品の需給がひっ迫して全土でガソリン不足が深刻
化して価格が押し上げられているほか、10 月１日からの公共料金の引き上げも物価を押し上げて
いる。燃料高は企業の輸送費などを通じて他の財・サービスにも波及する。中銀は、一部業種にお
ける生産能力の縮小を一時的な要因と位置付けつつ、その物価への影響が想定より長期化する可能
性を警戒している。

注１ ９月14 日付レポート「ウクライナの攻勢によるガソリン不足がロシア中銀の利下げにブレーキ」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/655069.html） 
図 1 インフレ率の推移

（出所）CEIC より第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

### Page 3

3/4 
資源輸出国であるロシアにとって、原
油高の影響は複雑である。中銀は、原油
などの輸出収入の増加が最近のルーブ
ル相場を支えたと説明している（図２）。
ルーブル相場の安定は、輸入物価の抑制
要因となる。一方で、前述の通りロシア
国内においては、製油所の操業障害が石
油製品の供給を制約している。このた
め、足元のロシアにおいては、資源輸出
収入の増加が対外収支の改善を促して
いるものの、国内の燃料高が併存する状
況に直面しており、インフレ圧力の解消は難しい状況にある。こうした事態を反映して、足元の期
待インフレ率や、企業部門による価格見通しは高止まりしている。企業の価格設定や賃金交渉に高
いインフレが織り込まれ、一時的な供給ショックの影響が長引くおそれがある。今回中銀が政策金
利を据え置いた背景には、需要の拡大を抑えながら、こうした二次的な波及を防ぐ狙いがあると考
えられる。

【低成長の下で高金利の長期化を迫られるなど、政策運営のハードルは高まっている】 
足元の景況感は、サービス業を中心に底堅い動きが確認されるとともに、先行きの投資活動の回
復を示唆する動きもみられる（図３）。し
かし、中銀が示した最新の経済成長率見
通しによれば、2026 年の経済成長率は＋
0.5 ～1.0 ％ と 従 来 見 通 し （ ＋0.0 ～
1.0％）から小幅に上方修正されたもの
の、依然として低い伸びにとどまる。さ
らに、2027 年も＋1.0～2.0％と従来見通
し（＋1.5～2.5％）から下方修正されて
いる。足元では人手不足感が徐々に緩和
する兆しがみられるものの、依然として
広範な分野で人手不足の懸念は残る。こ
のように供給能力が制約されるなかでは、低成長のもとでも物価上昇圧力が残りやすいことから、
景気の弱さだけを理由に利下げを進めることのハードルは高まっていると考えられる。 
財政運営面では、政府が９月末に連邦議会下院（国家院）に提出した 2027 年度予算案について
（注２）、 中銀は７月会合時点での想定に比べてやや慎重と評価したうえで、インフレ抑制に寄与

注２ 10 月２日付レポート「ロシア、財政運営はウクライナ戦争のさらなる長期化を鮮明に」

### Page 4

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
4/4 
するとの見方を示している。しかし、今年６月に連邦議会は予算法の改正を経ない形で歳出、債務
の拡大を可能にする法律を可決しており、国防費をはじめとする歳出を機動的に増加させることが
可能となっている。政府によれば、2026 年だけを対象とする特例措置としたものの、プーチン政権
を支える与党・統一ロシアは議会の３分の２を超える議席数を獲得しており、先行きもなし崩し的
に同様の対応が進む可能性には注意が必要である。このため、追加歳出などによって財政赤字が計
画を上回れば、需要を押し上げ、利下げ余地をさらに狭める可能性がある。財政健全化の計画が着
実に実行されるか否かの行方についても、金融政策の先行きを左右することが予想される。 
会合後に記者会見に臨んだ中銀のナビウリナ総裁は、当面の政策運営について「長期間にわたっ
て引き締め姿勢を維持する必要がある」との見方を示したうえで、今回の決定について「据え置き
案のみを実質的に議論した」と述べた。先行きについて「2027 年には利下げ余地が生まれる可能性
がある」とする一方、「インフレは想定に比べて高止まりするかもしれない」と述べるなど、その余
地が縮小しているとの認識を示す慎重姿勢をみせた。高金利の長期化は、借り入れを通じた需要拡
大を抑える一方、民間企業の資金調達や設備投資の負担にもなる。供給能力の回復や生産性の向上
には投資が必要であり、インフレ抑制を優先する政策と中長期の成長力確保との調整は難しい。今
回の予測改定は、物価目標への回帰に向けて、景気回復のペースにも慎重な見方が必要になってい
ることを示すものと考えられる。 
次回の金融政策決定会合は 12 月 18 日に予定されている。今後、生産能力の回復が進み、基調的
な物価上昇率と期待インフレが持続的に低下すれば、利下げ再開の余地は広がると考えられる。一
方で、供給障害が長引き、公共料金や燃料価格の上昇が他の物価に波及すれば、据え置きが長期化
すると予想される。2027 年の平均政策金利見通しは現行の 14％を下回っており、中銀は緩和余地
を残しているものの、その時期や幅は物価動向次第である。今後の焦点は、供給制約の緩和が期待
インフレの低下につながり、景気回復を支える利下げの余地をどこまで広げられるかにかかってい
る。 
以 上

（https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/666237.html）

2026 年 11 ⽉ 21 ⽇，中信建投新财富⽩⾦分析师、中信建投证券党委委员、执委会委员、机
